Among the first reports Marrow made to the breachers' centurion was of a gallery beneath the second gun deck that ran east and appeared on none of the Arsenal's plans.

### 2.8 The Rolling Up of the Outer Belt (Rotation Two, Cycles 305 to 380)

With more soldiers and tanks pushed into the breach at K-19, the Archflamen's army could no longer hold the outer belt. It had been built around its nests, and with its anchor taken, the Third's breachers could work along the outer belt's trenches from the inside, taking each nest in turn from the rear, where its guns did not bear. Prefect Ostorian sent his reserves north to stop them, drawing them out of their shelters on the Drum and in the Lethe Cut sector, and the sectors they left behind were thinly held. The Fourth Assault Phalanx went through those sectors and over seventy-five cycles rolled up the outer belt from K-19 to the Lethe Cut, with the Thureos tanks of the Sixteenth Armoured firing along the trench lines, the Hoplon carriers putting breachers onto the parapets behind their ramps, and the Kyklops carriages reducing the nests that held out by firing into their embrasures at fifty metres.

The Archflamen's army began to abandon the outer belt, falling back in good order, with the thrall cohorts covered by the garrison, to man the second belt thirty kilometres in, which was older and stronger and still being dug. The Third's outrider squadrons pursued them across the ash on their armoured hounds and caught the slower cohorts in the open. Several cohorts were left behind in the nests on the Drum, cut off by the roll-up, and the Seventh Line Phalanx took nine cycles to clear them. On the three hundred and eighty-first cycle of Rotation Two, the Répertoire logged the outer belt of the Pyriphlegethon Arsenal as reduced. The first objective of the siege had been won.

The reduction of the outer belt had been won by the methods the Third had trained for, and its lessons were drawn accordingly. The Répertoire's assessment credited the victory to the breakthrough at K-19 and to the speed with which the Fourth Assault Phalanx had exploited it, and recommended that every future assault be supported by armour held close enough to enter a breach within a cycle. It noted that the Archflamen's army had withdrawn in good order, with its garrison intact and most of its guns recovered to the second belt, and that the cost of the next belt should be estimated at twice that of the first. The Third's centurions drew a simpler conclusion. The outer belt had been the youngest and least finished of the three, and it had taken eighteen months. Nobody on the Flats believed that the Acropolis would be reached in Rotation Five.

It had cost nine hundred and sixty thousand transfers against an estimate of nine hundred thousand, and eighteen months of front time against an estimate of eight. Four thousand one hundred and forty of the transfers had been null, nearly twenty times the rate of the first assault, and the Répertoire's technical officers raised the figure with the Command, which noted it. Centurion Marrow was awarded the [[Corona Muralis]] for K-19, the first of the siege. In the Arsenal, the Archflamen held a liturgy for the dead of the outer belt in the nave of the Katabasion, at which their names were read over three cycles, and at its close he told the faithful that the Empire's dead came back and theirs did not, and asked them to consider which side the Imperator had chosen. He then sent Dace to the pens under the Tabularium to learn what else the strays had heard on the noosphere bands.

At Watchpoint Hades, the fall of the outer belt was received with relief and without enthusiasm. The Command had promised the theatre an end to the rationing within four rotations, and at the close of Rotation Two the Third stood before the second belt with two rotations of its timetable spent and the casting lines still in the Archflamen's hands. The rationing order was extended for a further three rotations. Divine batteries in the Lethe holds fell to four keyed rounds a cycle, and the Command began to move soldiers from the quieter sectors of the Acheron stairs to hold the holds that could no longer be held by shellfire. The College, which had kept a cautious silence since the attempt on the Archflamen, began to ask in its letters to the Command whether its proposal of a hearing for the Archflamen's doctrine might yet be considered, and was told that it might not. Castorre's standing with the Command was not yet in question, but his reports had begun to be read more closely.

### 2.9 The Second Belt (Rotation Three, Cycle 1)

On the first cycle of Rotation Three, the Third's regiments came up to the second belt along a front of one hundred and ten kilometres and began to dig in again.

The second belt was a different order of obstacle. It had been begun by the Arsenal a century before the revolt as a defence against the Phlegethon and had been extended and deepened by the thralls for two years, and it ran in an arc of one hundred and ten kilometres at about thirty kilometres from the spur. Where the outer belt had been a line of trenches gathered around nests, the second was a zone of fortification seven to eleven kilometres deep, with three trench lines, its own anti-tank ditches and minefields between them, and nests that had been built as permanent works with galleries beneath them linking one to the next. Ten of the sixty ceiling-lances stood within it in hardened silos, and its ground had been ranged by the Archflamen's gunners for longer than the Third had been on the Flats. Behind it lay the inner belt and the Cinder Wall. The Third had no plans of the second belt's newer works, and the Répertoire estimated that its reduction would take one rotation and cost one million eight hundred thousand transfers.

The Répertoire published a revised timetable the same cycle, adding two rotations to the campaign. Castorre signed it and sent it to the Command at Watchpoint Hades with a covering note stating that the Archflamen's army had been driven from its first line of defence with heavy losses, and that the Third would be before the Acropolis by the end of Rotation Five. He also moved the College's liturgy of thanksgiving to Rotation Six. It would be moved several more times before the siege was over.

## 3 The War of Attrition

> *"The Third does not count its dead. It counts the ones who do not come back. Learn the difference, and you will understand the Third."*
>
> Phalanx-General Oren Callix, Second Line Phalanx, to the Command at Watchpoint Hades

### 3.1 The Crisis of the Fifth (Rotation Three, Cycle 211)

Following the Third's breakthrough at K-19, the Archflamen's army abandoned not only the outer belt but also the northern spur, the unfinished arm of the second belt that ran along the lip of the Mora Cut. With the outer belt rolled up from the south, the spur was outflanked, and its defenders faced the Second and Fifth Line Phalanxes to their front, rested and refilled from the vats, while the Seventh Line Phalanx could cut behind them from the west. Rather than risk the loss of the garrison in an untenable position, Prefect Ostorian withdrew his cohorts to the main line of the second belt in the first week of Rotation Three. The [[Fifth Line Phalanx]] found the spur's trenches empty and advanced along the Cut against light resistance from rearguards, marksmen and the inevitable harassing fire, and by the ninetieth cycle its forward regiments had come around the end of the second belt and were digging in on its northern face. In more than two rotations of war, the Third had been on the attack at every point of the arc. On the two hundred and eleventh cycle of Rotation Three, as the Fifth closed upon the second belt from the north, the strategic position changed. The Archflamen's army came out of its trenches and launched the first planned offensive of the war.

The northern spur had been given up without a fight, but the ground between the spur and the second belt had been chosen in advance as the scene of a counter-attack. Ostorian's staff had identified the stretch of the Flats south of the spur where a regiment of the Fifth would have to halt and dig on its way in, ranged their guns upon it, and stocked the second belt behind it with tanks from the Arsenal's vaults and with Laistrygons. The [[Forty-First Trench Regiment]], under [[Brannoc Halde|Colonel Brannoc Halde]], was holding overnight positions there on Leaf 58-31, eleven kilometres short of the second belt. Through the night its sentries reported the sound of engines, and Halde requested harassing fire on the second belt to disrupt whatever was forming. Expecting a local counter-attack, he reinforced his forward cohorts and ordered a general stand-to at the first bell. The Archflamen's gunners opened with their customary scattering of rounds, and then every gun in the northern half of the second belt fired at once. The barrage fell upon the Forty-First's trenches for an hour, mixed with smoke shells whose clouds lay over the Flats at head height, and before it lifted the first wave of the attack emerged from the smoke.

The Thureos tanks of the Arsenal's vaults came first, firing into the Forty-First's crowded fire bays from four hundred metres, followed by Hoplon carriers along every cohort front of the regiment. Behind them came the thralls with bayonets, picks and casting-hooks, and in front of the thralls came the Laistrygons, driven forward at the speed of a horse by the stimulants of the Lictors. Rifle fire did little to stop them, and most reached the forward trench, where they went through the fire bays in packs while the thralls followed to finish what they had missed and the tanks drove up to the parapet to fire along the trench. One after another the Forty-First's forward positions were overrun. From his command dugout in the support line, Colonel Halde committed his reserve cohorts to stem the attack, and lost half of them to the barrage on their way forward. He requested reinforcements from the Fifth Line Phalanx and was told that none were available, since the Fifth's reserve regiments were engaged against attacks of their own on Leaves 61 and 62. He was to hold with what he had.

The fighting continued throughout the cycle, and by its end both armies' guns were firing on the Forty-First's support line, where the Third's soldiers and the Arsenal's forward cohorts were now intermingled. The Arsenal had overrun nineteen of the regiment's heavy mortar and quad-launcher pits, and in most of them had turned the guns upon the Forty-First until the ready ammunition was spent. By nightfall there was no longer a front line on Leaf 58-31, only pockets of each side in fragments of trench, with tanks moving between them and both sides probing in the dark. The attack was renewed at the first bell of the second cycle and reached the support line in its second hour. Halde and his staff held the command dugout against two assaults, but the trenches on either side were lost, and the dugout was isolated. His second, Tribune [[Senna Oake]], was killed when a tank fired through its observation slit. With eighty soldiers remaining and communications to the rear severed, Halde evacuated his wounded as best he could and divided the survivors. One group was to break out and carry word of the breakthrough to the Fifth, while Halde would hold the dugout with the other until relieved or overrun. He did not expect relief.

The breakout group escaped. Halde held the dugout through four further assaults until the Arsenal brought forward one of the crawlers its armourers had built in the first rotation of the siege from a Hoplon chassis and a heat-well slag projector, a machine able to throw molten slag at a thousand degrees across two hundred metres, which the thralls called a [[Pourer|pourer]]. It filled the dugout from forty metres. Nothing of Halde was ever recovered, since the slag set in the dugout like stone. The relay logs of the Fifth's section show that his implant fired at the seventy-first minute of the dark watch and that the upload was handed to the noosphere. It never reached Watchpoint Hades. Colonel Halde was entered as null on the two hundred and twelfth cycle of Rotation Three, the first officer of the Third above the rank of centurion to be lost in that way during the siege.

The pourers were the first of the weapons the Arsenal's armourers would build from the tools of the casting lines, and they were among the most feared. The Third's after-action reports counted eleven of them at Leaf 58-31, and they had destroyed more of the Forty-First's dugouts than the Arsenal's tanks. The Third had no answer to them beyond killing them at range before they reached the trenches, and in the rotations that followed every regiment on the line was issued with additional anti-armour weapons for the purpose. The Laistrygons presented a similar problem. The Third's rifles had been designed for the deep's lesser things and for the thralls, and a Laistrygon on combat stimulants could cross a hundred metres of ash under rifle fire and arrive in a fire bay still fighting. The Third's regiments responded by siting their heavy weapons for close defence and by issuing breachers' carbines to the forward cohorts, and the Répertoire added the Laistrygons to its estimate of the cost of every attack on the second belt.

With the loss of its command post, the Forty-First's support line was also broken. Isolated groups fought on without coordination, but as a regiment the Forty-First was a spent force, and in the confusion of the retreat most of its stores and heavy equipment were abandoned. The Forty-First had been the Fifth's left flank, and with it gone the whole of the Fifth's line along the second belt could be rolled up from the left. The enemy attack had to be stalled long enough for the Fifth's other regiments to turn and face it. The Fifth's Phalanx-General gathered the [[Twentieth Armoured Regiment]], on loan from the Fourth Assault Phalanx for the northern advance, and the Forty-First's surviving outrider squadrons, and threw both into the enemy's path. They would not stop it. The time they bought was what mattered. The Fifth's regiments were ordered to fall back, and the Forty-Fourth Trench Regiment of the Second Line Phalanx, on the Fifth's right, was ordered to swing its left back and dig in facing north to hold the hinge of the line. Centurion Marrow's Sixth Cohort dug a kilometre of that hinge in a cycle and a half under fire, and held it.

On the third cycle the Arsenal's tanks pressed on, but the Fifth had by then turned enough of its strength to meet them. The Twentieth Armoured had forty-eight tanks on the line, Thureos tanks and a squadron of old Ajax heavies, against more than a hundred and twenty enemy tanks by the outriders' reports, a number that seemed to grow with every report. Over the next three cycles the Twentieth's tank commanders fought a series of delaying actions back across the Flats, from one cinder rise to the next, and with each cycle the Arsenal took less ground. On the sixth cycle of the counter-attack its armour was spent, the Laistrygons had exhausted their stimulants, and the attack came to a halt. It had driven a wedge eleven kilometres deep through the Fifth's line and forced it to give up most of the ground it had taken since the fall of the outer belt. The Forty-First Trench Regiment had been destroyed in two cycles, with seventy-six thousand of its soldiers transferred or captured, its colonel among them. Four thousand two hundred of the transfers were null. The Arsenal's losses had also been heavy, above all in armour, and the Twentieth Armoured counted ninety-one enemy hulls on the Flats when the fighting was over. The Répertoire put the Archflamen's losses at forty-one thousand and judged the exchange favourable.

Along the arc, it was difficult to see it that way. The attacks that should have weakened the Archflamen's army had only emboldened it. It had shown that it could leave its trenches and hurt the Third, and that its gunners could read the Third's movements and be ready for them. The second belt would be far more difficult to break than had been planned.

The Command ordered an inquiry into the loss of the Forty-First, which reported within the rotation. It found that the Fifth Line Phalanx had advanced on the second belt faster than its guns could follow, and that its regiments had halted to dig on ground the enemy had ranged in advance and could reach with every gun in the northern half of his line. It found that the sound of engines through the night had been reported to the Fifth's headquarters and treated as a local matter. It found that the Third had no reliable estimate of the armour in the Arsenal's vaults and had assumed that the garrison would not risk it outside the belts. On all three counts the inquiry was correct, and it was careful to add that each decision had been reasonable on what was known at the time. Its recommendations were adopted. The Third's advance would henceforth halt each cycle only within the cover of its own guns, every regiment on the line would keep armour within a cycle's drive, and the Third's spotters were ordered to treat any movement of vehicles behind the second belt as the preparation of an attack. The inquiry did not consider Colonel Halde's null, which it noted as a consequence of the slag that had destroyed his dugout, and which the Third's technical officers listed with the other nulls of the counter-attack as an effect of the Archflamen's jammers.

The Archflamen's offensive also changed the way the Third's soldiers regarded their enemy. Until the counter-attack the thralls had been seen on the line as a mass to be killed in its trenches, armed with the Arsenal's rifles and driven by its Lictors, and the war as a contest between the Third's guns and the Arsenal's concrete. After Leaf 58-31 the Third knew that the Arsenal could attack, that its gunners and its tank crews were good, and that the Laistrygons in the open were a weapon the Third had no answer to except heavy fire at close range. The Third's regiments began to dig their forward trenches deeper and their fire bays narrower, and to site their heavy guns to fire along the front of their own wire. The Répertoire increased its estimate of the cost of the second belt by half.

### 3.2 The Strays (Rotation Three, Cycle 214)

It was in the aftermath of the counter-attack that Haruspex Dace made the discovery upon which the Archflamen's war would come to depend. The Hecatean strays had by then been moved from the pens beneath the Tabularium into its old guest quarters, where the Archflamen provided for them from his own table, and Dace spent most nights among them asking what they had heard. Most of them heard the river, and some claimed to hear the Imperator. A few heard the noosphere. The most sensitive was a girl of eleven named [[Mirren]], who described its traffic as a flock of birds passing high overhead without pause. On the night after the destruction of the Forty-First, she told Dace that a great flight of birds had passed that morning, and that one of them had been loud and calling out a number. Dace checked the number against the codes of the Third's officers, taken from the Tabularium's files on the night it fell, and found it was Colonel Halde's. He asked the girl to try to call such a bird down when the next one passed. It is the first recorded instance of the work that would become the Echo Mast.

Dace's interest in the strays had begun as a matter of intelligence. The Third's officers spoke to one another over the relay bands, which the Arsenal's signallers could jam but not read, and the strays who heard the noosphere could tell him when the Third's traffic grew heavy before an attack, and on which stretch of the line. Through Rotations Three and Four, the Arsenal's warning of the Third's assaults came as often from the guest quarters of the Tabularium as from its own sentries, and Ostorian, who was told that the warnings came from prayer, learned to trust them. Halde's upload showed Dace that the strays could hear more than traffic. An upload in the noosphere carried the pattern of a soldier, and a stray who could hear it could tell whose it was and, in time, what it contained. Dace kept the strays at the work for many hours each cycle, and some of them sickened under it. He replaced them from among the thralls' children, in whom the witch-blood showed more often than the Arsenal's registry had ever recorded, and by the end of Rotation Five the strays in his care numbered more than five hundred.

### 3.3 The Eye of Light and CODE 78E (Rotation Four, Cycle 3)

The Third recovered the lost ground more slowly than it had lost it. In the north, the Fifth Line Phalanx was refilled from the vats and from the depot at Kappa, and a regiment the Command had held back on the ceiling against a reformation peak further up the Phlegethon came down the Stair to take the Forty-First's place. The Forty-First's own soldiers, returning from Watchpoint Hades by the thousand, were posted to other regiments, since their regiment no longer existed. The Fifth went forward again against the second belt in the manner it had used against the outer belt, digging under the guns metre by metre.

On the third cycle of Rotation Four, the campaign was interrupted by events far beyond the Cinder Flats. Two hundred kilometres to the north, at the Phlegethon's upper bend at Depth 38, a burning object came down out of the ceiling on a long slant and struck the deep at [[Grid Delta]], Sector Nineteen-F. The impact was felt along the whole of the arc, from the Mora Cut to the Demus Gorge. A column of fire kilometres high stood over the northern skyline for seven minutes, and its glow did not fade for a rotation. Within the hour the Répertoire had issued the registry of the vessel. It was the [[Eye of Light]], the [[BATTLESHIPS|battleship]] whose capture by the [[Order of the Hollow Crescent]] and recovery at Luna are recorded in Rex Invictus VIII ([[Ch15 - Rex Invictus VIII|Chapter 15]]), and which had since been requisitioned from inspection at the [[Forge of Hephaestus]] by the [[Alpha-Class AI|Alpha-Class]] entity [[Minerva (AI)|Minerva]] and flown into Tartarus.

> RÉPERTOIRE DU SAVOIR ATHÉNIEN. FLEET REGISTRY. EXTRACT.

> AC-09-1642. THE EYE OF LIGHT. ANNCHASE-CLASS BATTLESHIP, 1,642ND OF ITS PATTERN.
> BUILT: FORGE OF HEPHAESTUS. ASSIGNED: 9TH OLYMPIAN REACH, LOCAL DEFENCE FORCE, UNDER THE HOUSE OF APOLLO.
> STATUS: LOST WITH ALL HANDS. CAPTURED BY THE ORDER OF THE HOLLOW CRESCENT, THIRTY-TWO YEARS PRIOR TO THIS ENTRY. RECOVERED AT LUNA ON THE HERETICS' WITHDRAWAL. REQUISITIONED FROM SCHEDULED INSPECTION AT THE FORGE OF HEPHAESTUS BY AN ALPHA-CLASS ENTITY, IDENTIFIED: MINERVA.
> TERMINAL EVENT: IMPACT, TARTARUS, DEPTH 38, GRID DELTA, SECTOR NINETEEN-F. VESSEL EXPENDED AS A WAYPOINT.
> ESTIMATED EFFECT: NEUTRALISATION OF 0.09 PERCENT OF ACTIVE TARTARUS MACROFORMS IN THEATRE.

Within minutes of the registry, the Répertoire issued a general verdict to every hold and quarterdeck in the theatre, the first occasion in the Third's history on which its voice was broadcast directly to the soldiers of the line rather than read to them by their officers.

The broadcast was without precedent, and it was felt along the whole of the Third's line. Soldiers who had served for decades on the Flats had known the Répertoire only through the verdicts their officers read aloud and the figures by which their attacks were priced. To hear it address them directly, in a voice they could not mistake for any officer's, unsettled many of them more than the impact itself. The Third's Flamens reported that attendance at the vat-rites rose for a month afterwards, and the Third's provosts reported that a number of soldiers in the forward cohorts had begun to address their prayers to the Répertoire, a practice the College condemned and the Command chose not to notice.

> "Verdict. Accepted. Warzone Re-evaluation, [[CODE 78E]]. Every and all mortal combat assets initiate total assault. Penetrate the Tartarus front at Grid Delta, Sector Nineteen-F. Utilize Battleship AC-09-1642 as waypoint. All artillery, rotary, and missile batteries, continuous fire saturation ahead of advancing infantry. Full authorization granted for Heavy Armor brigades, breakthrough formation, bypass all secondary contacts. Ave Imperator."

> "Clarification. This will establish a permanent territorial wedge for the first time since the Second Grand Offensive. All auxiliary garrisons will reinforce from rear sectors. ETA: 15 hours. Good Luck."

The verdict placed Lord-General Castorre in a difficult position. The Third was engaged in a siege two hundred kilometres from Grid Delta, and its plan required a rotation of sapping before the second belt could be attacked. A second leaf addressed to the Third's command settled the matter. Two Trench Regiments and the reserve of the Fourth Assault Phalanx were to go up the ceiling relays to [[Wedge (Grid Delta)|the Wedge]] at once, to reinforce from the rear, and the remainder of the Third was to make a general attack on the second belt along its whole front under continuous saturation fire, so that nothing on the Cinder Flats that answered to the Archflamen, in the trenches or in the river, would be free to move north while the Wedge was driven in. Castorre's chief of staff, Leda Corvis, pointed out that the Third had in fact reached the second belt on twelve leaves and was within two kilometres of it on the rest, and that the Répertoire's own figure for the attack was the six regiments the Third could spare. Castorre signed the order. In six hundred years no Lord-General of the Third who had disputed a [[CODE 78 VARIANTS|CODE 78]] had kept his command for a rotation afterwards. He then asked who had crashed the ship. Corvis could tell him only what the registry said, and advised that the Wedge command at Grid Delta belonged to Lord Commander [[Varric von Romhardt]], who would be the officer most likely to know. Castorre requested a line to Varric's base and was informed that it was empty, since every officer and runner in it had gone forward with the assault.

The Wedge at Grid Delta belongs to the history of the Tartarus front at large, and its course is recorded elsewhere. For the Third, its significance lay in what it demanded of the siege and in what it revealed of the Third's place in the Répertoire's calculations. The verdict had treated the Third as an auxiliary garrison of the Wedge, to be drawn upon for soldiers and spent to hold the enemy in place, and the Third's own objectives on the Cinder Flats had not been weighed against the Wedge's at all. Castorre's staff understood this at once, and so did the Phalanx-Generals. Callix of the Second protested in writing that a general attack on an unreduced belt without a fire plan would cost the Third a rotation of its timetable and give the Archflamen a victory he had not earned, and asked that his phalanx be allowed to make its attack by sapping. The Répertoire's reply restated the verdict. The general attack would go in along the whole front as ordered.

### 3.4 The Saturation (Rotation Four, Cycles 3 to 9)

That night the Twenty-Second and Thirty-Third Trench Regiments went up the Stair with the reserve of the Fourth Assault Phalanx, one hundred and eighty thousand soldiers and two hundred tanks, and were on the ceiling relays to Grid Delta by the end of the next watch. The Third never recovered the Thirty-Third. It went into the Wedge at Sector Nineteen-F with Varric's assault and remained there four rotations later, holding a section of the line that the Répertoire had declared permanent, and which proved to be so.

On the fifth cycle of Rotation Four, six regiments of the Third attacked the second belt along the line from the Mora Cut to the Lethe Cut, after nine hours of fire that the Third's gunners afterwards called [[The Saturation|the Saturation]]. Every gun on the Flats fired without pause and, as the verdict required, without a fire plan, and two Phaethons split their barrels from the rate of fire. In most places the attack was stopped at the wire, which had been laid over two rotations and was deeper than that of the outer belt, while the nests behind it were older, harder and largely untouched, since the Saturation had not been aimed at them. The Third's dead lay in heaps before the second belt. Elsewhere the attack broke into the forward trenches and the support trenches behind them, only to stall under the counter-barrage. The Archflamen's gunners had ranged on their own forward trenches for a rotation in anticipation of such a day, and their fire isolated the attackers in their new positions until the garrison cohorts came up from their shelters and drove them out before they could be reinforced. The attack continued in this manner for six cycles, each wave repulsed and followed by the next like the ebb and flow of a tide, and each cycle the attack was widened to a neighbouring leaf to eliminate its flanking fire, and failed there too.

For the Archflamen's army, the Saturation was the most severe trial of the war so far, and it was survived by the method Ostorian had adopted after the first assault. His soldiers spent nine hours of preparation and six cycles of attack in the deep shelters of the second belt, coming up only to man their guns when the Third's infantry crossed the wire, and his losses, though heavy, fell chiefly on the forward trenches he had expected to lose. The Arsenal put them at sixty thousand. The Archflamen's preachers told the thralls that the Empire had thrown every gun it had at the Imperator's Hold and that the Hold still stood, and it was the truth. When the Third's attack ended, the Arsenal's garrison cohorts went forward and reoccupied every metre of the second belt but the six hundred the Third had kept, and the thralls began repairing the wire the same night.

On the ninth cycle the Répertoire lifted CODE 78E for the Cinder Flats. The Wedge at Grid Delta had held, and the Répertoire's message to the Third stated that the Archflamen's army had been fixed in place as required and that the general attack had achieved its object. It had gained three hundred metres on two leaves at a cost of three hundred and twelve thousand transfers, eleven thousand of which were null.

The Saturation was the most costly six cycles of the siege to that date, and for little purpose that the Third could see. Its shell expenditure was larger still. The Third fired more than eleven million shells in nine hours of preparation and six cycles of attack, a quarter of its stocks on the Flats, and much of that fire fell on ground that held nothing. The keyed ammunition the Third had drawn from the Arsenal's lists before the revolt was not replaced, and after the Saturation the Third's divine batteries were put on a ration for the first time in the siege. The null rate of the attack, three and a half percent of all transfers, was the first to exceed the ceiling set in the order of assignment, and the Répertoire's technical officers reported it to the Command, which asked them to account for it. They attributed it to the Archflamen's jammers and to the density of the fire, which had destroyed more of the Third's relay pylons in six cycles than in the whole of the preceding rotation.

The Saturation also cost Castorre the confidence of his own officers. He had signed the order without protest, and the Third's centurions, who did not know of the verdict's terms or of the second leaf, held him responsible for the general attack and for its failure. Callix's written protest became known along the line, and was copied and passed from cohort to cohort until the Third's provosts were ordered to confiscate it. The Command at Watchpoint Hades, for its part, had begun to doubt whether the Third's commander could break the second belt at all. No one suggested that he be replaced. The Third had never had its Lord-General relieved during a campaign, and the Command was not yet ready to be the first to do it.

### 3.5 The Long Stalemate (Rotation Four, Cycle 9 to Rotation Five, Cycle 400)

After the Saturation, the Third returned to sapping. The Mattocks and the cohorts dug forward under the guns, and by the end of Rotation Four the Flats inside the old outer belt had become a labyrinth of saps, parallels and communication trenches in which the Third's own runners lost their way. The second belt was attacked hundreds of times over the following year of front time. Wherever a regiment took a nest, the counter-barrage cut it off and the garrison retook it. The Archflamen's raiders took the Third's forward saps in turn and filled them with the Third's dead. There was no breakthrough, and another rotation passed in stalemate.

In the north, the Fifth Line Phalanx fared better. Refilled once more and fighting in ground the Archflamen had not had time to fortify, between the old northern spur and the main line of the second belt, it found that the Arsenal's cohorts were exposed to the Third's guns whenever they left their deep shelters to fight. Slowly it recovered the ground lost in the counter-attack, though it took six months to retake what had been lost in a week. In the last month of that advance it reached the thrall-town at [[Ore Head]], a settlement of forty thousand prefabricated huts at the northern end of the second belt where the Arsenal had housed its ore-lift gangs. After a month of shelling, the Fifth took it almost without a fight. Nothing remained of the huts. By the end of Rotation Five the Third's line ran around the whole of the second belt from the Mora Cut to the Lethe Cut, and the Arsenal was encircled on a front of one hundred and nine kilometres. During its advance the Third had overrun most of the Arsenal's outer stores, the ammunition dumps and ration depots kept on the Flats for its patrols, and found every one of them stripped. Their contents had been moved closer to the Acropolis, down to the last round.

The Third adapted to the long stalemate in the manner of a trench army. Its regiments were rotated through the line on a cycle of sixty days in the forward trenches and thirty in the support line and the rest camps behind the railheads, and the Third's medical officers began to keep a register of soldiers who had been transferred more than twenty times, who were to be posted out of the forward trenches altogether. The register grew faster than the rest camps. By the end of Rotation Five more than a fifth of the Third's soldiers on the Flats had passed twenty transfers, and some regiments could fill their forward cohorts only by ignoring it. Phalanx-General Callix, who counted the nulls of the Second Line Phalanx by hand at the end of every rotation, found that the Second's null rate had risen from under one part in a thousand in Rotation One to more than three parts in a hundred in Rotation Five. He reported the figure to the Command with the observation that the Third did not count its dead but counted those who did not come back, and that the second number was the one the Third should be watching. The Command acknowledged the report.

The war of attrition continued to consume soldiers and equipment every cycle. The artillery duel never ceased, and the ground between the lines became a field of craters so dense that a soldier could pass from one to the next without touching level ground. Wherever the Third made gains, the Archflamen's counter-attacks recovered them, and both sides harassed the other so constantly with mortars, raids and marksmen that neither was allowed a moment's rest. For months at a time the front did not move. The Third's commanders wanted a breakthrough. For the time being, they had attrition.

Beneath the stalemate, the Third's null rate was rising. The list of nulls posted each rotation in the recovery halls at Watchpoint Hades had once filled a single sheet. By Rotation Five the list for the Cinder Flats alone ran to four metres, and the soldiers who read it on their way back to the line were the first in the Third to say openly that something on the Flats was catching them on the way up. The clerks of the recovery halls began to ask returning soldiers whether their missing comrades had died near a relay pylon. The question had no answer the Third could act upon.

The Répertoire's technical officers worked on the problem through Rotation Five without result. They established that the nulls of the Cinder Flats were not distributed as interference would distribute them. They were heavier in the northern Flats than in the south, heavier at night than by day, and heavier in assaults on the second belt than in the ordinary business of the line, and they did not follow the loss of pylons as closely as the Third's earlier figures had suggested. The technical officers asked for a survey of the noosphere bands over the spur, which could be made only from the ceiling inside the lance envelope, and were told that no ship could be risked there. They asked for the Hunt's assistance in examining the bands, and were told that the Hunt did not take requests from the Répertoire. Their interim report of the four hundredth cycle concluded that the cause of the excess nulls was unknown and that the Arsenal's Echo Mast, which had been drawing power since the revolt, should be considered a possible factor. The Command noted the report and took no action on it.

### 3.6 The Arsenal Besieged

Inside the ring, the Arsenal had been transformed by four rotations of war. The casting lines worked without pause, under shellfire from the Phaethons whenever the Third's spotters could find them, and the Arsenal's production of keyed shells, which had been the theatre's whole supply before the revolt, now went entirely to the Archflamen's own guns, which therefore fired with a freedom the Third's rationed batteries could not match. The thralls who were not in the belts worked the heat-wells and the vaults in shifts of sixteen hours. They were fed from the reserve stocks at a scale above anything they had known under the thrall-masters, which did more to keep them loyal than any sermon, and the Lictors of the Flame kept the rest in order. The Lictors' punishments were public and frequent. A thrall who left his post under fire was shot on the spot, a cohort that broke was decimated, and by the Arsenal's own records some forty thousand of the Archflamen's people were executed by his Lictors in the first five rotations of the siege.

The Archflamen himself was seldom seen. He preached in the nave of the Katabasion on the first cycle of each rotation, read the names of the faithful dead, and otherwise kept to the reliquary and his prayers, leaving the war to Ostorian and the Arsenal to Dace. His sermons in these years show a man increasingly certain of his calling and increasingly uninterested in the means by which it was served, and the Third's intelligence officers, who obtained copies through prisoners and deserters, assessed him as a figurehead whose death would change nothing. The assessment was half correct. He governed nothing, but the Arsenal's six million fought for him, and the Hunt's later inquiry concluded that without the Archflamen the Arsenal's thralls would have surrendered within a rotation of the revolt.

### 3.7 The Raid on Ceiling-Lance Seven (Rotation Five, Cycle 402)

On the four hundred and second cycle of Rotation Five, the Twenty-Ninth Trench Regiment of the [[Ninth Line Phalanx]] made a raid in strength on Leaf 50-27. Its objective was to capture and destroy Ceiling-Lance Seven, one of the ten lances in the second belt, a Phlegethon-fed emitter in a silo cut forty metres into the basalt and protected on the surface by razor-wire, a dense minefield and four gun emplacements. The Répertoire wanted it destroyed. With Lance Seven gone, a corridor would open through the lance envelope above the northern Flats, and the Tartarus Fleet could bring a heavy cruiser down into it whose main guns would accomplish in a cycle what the Phaethons accomplished in a month.

The lances were the key to the whole siege, and the Third had known it from the beginning. While they stood, the Archflamen could be fought only from the ground, by an army whose guns fired at ranges the Phaethons barely reached and whose every shell came down the Cinder Stair. Once enough of them were destroyed, the Tartarus Fleet could bring its heavy cruisers into the geometry above the Flats and fire on the belts and the spur at will, and the siege would become a matter of rotations rather than years. The Arsenal knew it too. The ten lances of the second belt were the most heavily defended points in it, each in its own nest of emplacements and wire, and the Archflamen's gunners had ranged on the ground around every one of them. The Répertoire had chosen Lance Seven because it stood nearest to the Third's line on the northern Flats, where the corridor it opened would serve the Fifth Line Phalanx's advance.

To preserve surprise, the raid would go in without a bombardment. Two hundred and sixty breachers, without their plate and with their faces blacked with cinder, would infiltrate across no-man's land in the dark and seize the silo's surface works at the first bell, before descending into the silo to destroy the lance. On their signal, the Twenty-Ninth's cohorts would advance in daylight to occupy the ground around it.

The breachers went out at the start of the dark watch and crawled from crater to crater through the mud left by a seep-storm two cycles before, lying still among the dead whenever a flare burst overhead. One section encountered an Arsenal patrol halfway across, and the fight that followed was taken by the sentries on the second belt for the usual business of the dark watch. Seven sections reached the wire around Lance Seven before the third hour and had cut through the first belt when a flare burst directly overhead and a sentry in the nearest emplacement saw them. With the garrison of the surface works roused, the breachers pressed forward through the wire and against the emplacements to avoid being destroyed where they lay, and for the rest of the night Leaf 50-27 was lit by the fighting. At the first bell, the silo was hidden in smoke and no signal had been received, for the breachers' bands were jammed. The Ninth Line Phalanx ordered the attack to continue as planned, on the Répertoire's estimate that the surface works had probably been taken. The Twenty-Ninth's cohorts went over the top toward the smoke.

The breachers had not taken the surface works. The Arsenal's gunners on the second belt saw the cohorts coming and turned Leaf 50-27 into a slaughterhouse, until the Twenty-Ninth's colonel called the survivors back across ground covered with their own dead. The next cycle, the Third's spotters found the surface works scorched and two emplacements wrecked. The emitter was intact, and at the change of the second watch it fired on a [[Stymphalian Swarm|Stymphalian]] swarm passing over the northern Flats, as it had every cycle for eight thousand years. Six hundred and forty soldiers of the Twenty-Ninth had been transferred in the raid. Of the two hundred and sixty breachers, nineteen returned. The Répertoire listed thirty-one as taken.

The thirty-one reappeared four cycles later, tied upright to the wire stakes in front of Lance Seven, three hundred metres from the Twenty-Ninth's forward trench. The Ninth Line Phalanx refused permission for a rescue, on the Répertoire's assessment that any attempt would end in a second massacre for the sake of soldiers who would be transferred in any case. The Twenty-Ninth's medicus disputed the assessment. The breachers had been kept alive, and each of them bore a dressing at the base of the skull, where the implant sat. They died on the wire one at a time over the following week. Each time one of them died, the Twenty-Ninth's relay operators logged the implant firing and the upload entering the noosphere. None of the thirty-one reached Watchpoint Hades. Their bodies remained on the wire for a rotation afterwards. The Répertoire's technical officers reported to the Command at Watchpoint Hades that the Archflamen's forces had found a means to interfere with the transfer of captured soldiers through work on the implant, that the work was beyond the competence of a forge-hold's armourers, and asked whether the Arsenal was receiving help. The Command noted the report.

The failure of the raid closed the Third's hopes of naval support for the second belt. The Tartarus Fleet had held a heavy cruiser at readiness in the low geometry above Ore Head for three cycles before the raid, waiting for the corridor the destruction of Lance Seven would open, and Navarch Saturnin withdrew it the cycle after the raid, with a note to the Command that the Fleet would not bring ships into the envelope until the Third could guarantee a corridor. The fate of the thirty-one became known along the whole of the arc within a rotation, and the Third's soldiers drew their own conclusions from it. Raids for prisoners continued, but soldiers on the line began to carry a last grenade for themselves, a practice the Third's officers forbade and its centurions ignored.

At the close of Rotation Five, the reduction of the Pyriphlegethon Arsenal had been in progress for five rotations of the theatre's clock, against a plan of four, and the Third stood before the second belt with the second belt still unbroken. The Répertoire's running total for the siege stood at a little over five million transfers, of which about one hundred and ninety thousand were null. The Arsenal's casting lines were intact and in the Archflamen's hands, and the rest of the theatre had been on rationed shells for five rotations. The Lethe holds had given up three of their sectors to the deep, the Acheron stairs one, and the Command had drawn eleven regiments from quieter fronts to hold what remained. On every measure the Répertoire kept, the siege was failing, and the Third's commander had not yet been told so in writing. The Archflamen, meanwhile, was about to receive help from the river.

### 3.8 The Katabasion (Rotation Five, Cycle 411)

The answer to the technical officers' question was already in the Arsenal. On the night the last of the thirty-one died, Dace reported to the Archflamen that Mirren had drawn the final breacher's upload down from the noosphere and held it for nearly a minute before it escaped. He then set out the principle on which the Echo Mast would be built. An upload passed seven seconds in the open between the implant and the first relay. A stray who could hold one for a minute could hold one until it was empty. The doctrine he offered the Archflamen in justification would later be known as the [[Kindlers]]' creed. The Imperator had gone down into the dark and come back up through it, while the Empire's soldiers had forgotten what the dark was and treated their deaths as a lift between the line and the vats, and the Imperator wanted them to remember. Dace also told the Archflamen that there were others in the river who wanted the same thing and had been waiting to be asked, and that he had asked them. The Archflamen made no recorded reply.

Four cycles later, a picket of the Fifth Line Phalanx on the lip of the Mora Cut reported a column of soldiers climbing out of the Cut on the old ore road below Ore Head, from the direction of the Styx. They wore the Empire's armour, and their transponders answered with the Empire's codes.

## 4 The Forsworn and the Cohort of Nemesis

> *"An oath is a debt. Nemesis collects."*
>
> Inscription over the gate of the Nemesis cohort-house at Rhamnous Primaris

### 4.1 A New Threat (Rotation Six, Cycle 1)

By the start of Rotation Six, the Third had been on the Cinder Flats for seven rotations of front time, counting the build-up. In that time it had transferred something over five million soldiers to take two belts of trenches and a few hundred metres of a third, while the Archflamen's army had lost more than a million of its own, permanently. Each side had won victories and suffered defeats, and neither was close to breaking. Lord-General Castorre requested from the Command at Watchpoint Hades a new Line Phalanx for the Cinder Flats. He believed that one more Phalanx, committed on the western Flats, would break the second belt before the end of the rotation and bring the campaign, already running longer and costing far more than the Répertoire had planned, back onto its timetable. The request was passed through the ordinary channels of the [[Dionysian Logistics Directorate]], whose clerks set to work searching the depots for three Trench Regiments to which no other theatre had a claim.

The request arrived at a poor moment. The theatre's shell shortage had not eased since the Arsenal fell, and every hold from the Acheron stairs to the Lethe was asking the Command for soldiers to replace the divine batteries it could no longer feed. The Directorate's clerks were obliged to weigh the Cinder Flats against those claims, and the Flats did not compare well. The Third had been given four rotations to take the Arsenal and had used six without reaching the inner belt. Its transfers had exceeded every estimate the Répertoire had made, and the vat capacity at Watchpoint Hades it consumed was capacity denied to the holds that were still holding. Within the Command there were officers who argued that the Third should be held where it stood, the Arsenal left to starve inside its belts and the soldiers sent where they would do more good. Castorre's family on the Pontifical College argued the opposite, and the College had its own reasons for doing so. An Archflamen in revolt was the College's embarrassment before it was anyone else's, and the College wanted him removed while the faithful of the holds still regarded him as a priest who had gone astray rather than as a martyr. The request went back and forth between the Directorate, the College and the Command for most of a rotation. While it did so the Third fought on with what it had, and the Archflamen's army was given time to make use of the help that was already on its way to it.

The siege then took a turn the Third had not foreseen. On the four hundred and fifteenth cycle of Rotation Five, a picket of the Twenty-Sixth Trench Regiment, posted at the head of the old ore road where it climbed the face of the Mora Cut below Ore Head, reported a column of about four hundred soldiers coming up out of the Cut from the direction of the Styx. Nothing had used the ore road in four rotations. The column wore the Empire's grey trench armour and the black cloaks of the Styx Watch, and its transponders answered the picket's challenge with the codes of a cohort of the [[Ninth Styx Legion]], which had held the confluence at Depth 44 for a thousand years. Its commander, a woman with a centurion's crest, explained that the cohort had been cut off at the confluence when the river rose in Rotation Two and had fought its way up the Cut for four rotations toward the sound of the guns. The picket's optio checked her codes against the Répertoire and found them valid, since the Ninth Styx Legion had indeed lost contact with a cohort of that number in Rotation Two. The column was passed through, logged on the picket's slate and given a stretch of reserve trench on Leaf 52-33, behind the Forty-Fourth Trench Regiment, while the Command decided what to do with it. By the next cycle it was gone. The Fifth Line Phalanx's staff assumed it had been sent up the Stair to rejoin its Legion. Nobody checked.

The column had gone on through the second belt by the ore road beneath Ore Head and presented itself to the Archflamen. Its commander met him in the Katabasion on the second cycle of Rotation Six, and the Tabularium's record of the meeting, recovered after the siege, is the only account the [[Forsworn]] ever gave of themselves. She gave her name as [[Hesper Unsworn|Hesper]]. She had once been a Legate of the Ninth Styx Legion and had sworn its oath on the Styx at nineteen, as every soldier of the Ninth did, to hold the confluence until the Imperator came back up through it. She held it for six hundred years, until the Command ordered the Ninth to give up a third of the confluence to save a hold upriver. She refused, was relieved, and went down into the Styx with the cohorts that would follow her sooner than break the oath. The river had taken them. By her account the Styx kept an oath broken from above and took the person who had sworn it, and the Forsworn were what remained, the only soldiers who still held the Ninth's oath. They had long since cut out their implants. The Empire's soldiers, she said, went up when they died and came back down and believed it a gift, while the Forsworn had seen where the uploads went on the way. She told the Archflamen that his Haruspex had asked the river for help, that the Forsworn had heard, that others had heard as well, and that the Forsworn had been closest. What they wanted was what the Archflamen wanted. The Empire's soldiers were to remember what dying was.

Hesper offered the Archflamen the four hundred she had brought and two thousand more who would come up the Cut when she called them. She offered training for his thralls, whose fighting she had watched for a cycle from the ore road and judged poor, and her own six hundred years of trench war on the Styx. She also offered something she would not describe before his clerks, which Dace afterwards took her to see the strays about. The Archflamen accepted. Dace had told him for two rotations that the river would answer if asked, and he had not believed it.

### 4.2 The Forsworn

The force Hesper placed at the Archflamen's disposal was small in number and out of all proportion to its size in effect. The Répertoire's later reconstruction put the Forsworn on the spur at no more than two thousand four hundred at any time, organised in the old cohorts of the Ninth Styx Legion and still bearing their numbers. They had been soldiers of the Empire for six centuries before they were anything else, and they knew the Third's drill, its codes, its relay bands and its habits as well as its own centurions did. The river had changed them. They did not tire and did not appear to need rations, they could cross ground under fire that would have stopped a cohort of the Third, and wounds that would have sent a soldier of the Third to the vats did not stop them. Their armour was the Ninth's grey trench plate, over which a long immersion in the Styx had laid something the Third's rifles could not penetrate. Their weapons were the black swords of [[Stygian Iron|Styx-iron]], which the Ninth had carried at the confluence against the things that came up the river, and whose wounds the implant could not save a soldier from. The Forsworn had no implants of their own. A Forsworn killed on the Flats was gone, as a thrall was, and Hesper spent them with care.

They were most dangerous as instructors and as infiltrators. The Archflamen's army was large, well supplied and fanatical, and until Rotation Six it had fought like an army of forge-workers. Its thrall cohorts held their bays bravely and died in them, and its counter-attacks came on cue and in the same places, so that the Third's gunners had learned to expect them. Hesper changed that within two rotations. Under the Forsworn the thralls learned to give up their forward trenches to the Third's assault waves and retake them before the next waves arrived, to hold their shelters until the counter-barrage had isolated the Third's lodgements, and to strike at the junctions between the Third's regiments, where orders took longest to travel. The Sworn of Orrhae learned to raid at night in small parties under the Forsworn's eye, and the Third's losses among centurions and signallers on the second belt doubled in Rotation Six. The Forsworn themselves moved behind the Third's lines in the Empire's armour, answering challenges with the Empire's codes, and for most of Rotation Six the Third had no means of telling them from its own soldiers. No regiment of the Third was told of their presence until the end of the rotation.

### 4.3 The Call of the Mast

The Echo Mast's broadcast was the Forsworn's most consequential contribution to the Archflamen's war, and the Third was the last to understand it. The Mast had been built eight thousand years before as the noosphere relay of a forge-hold and as a beacon for the holds the Arsenal had once supplied below Depth 40. Its downward bands had not been used since those holds were abandoned. Hesper's signallers knew the bands from the Ninth's own archive, and the nine cycles of broadcast they made on them in Rotation Six carried the Archflamen's war down every river that met the Phlegethon below the Flats. The Répertoire tracked what answered. It logged movement at Depth 43, where the Lethe met the Phlegethon, and under the [[Cocytus]] ice at Depth 47, and along the lower Phlegethon below the Arsenal's old docks, in formations the Third's spotters had never recorded moving together before. The Répertoire assessed the movements as an ordinary reformation cycle of the deep and assigned them to the holds downriver. It did not connect them to the carrier tone on the Third's relay sets. The connection was made only in Rotation Eight, when what had answered reached the Cinder Flats.

The Forsworn were put to work at once. They went into the second belt with the thrall cohorts and taught them to fight in the bays, to wait in the shelters until the barrage lifted and to counter-attack before the Third could bring its next wave across. They trained the best of the Sworn of Orrhae as raiders and sent them through the Third's lines at night to kill officers and signallers in their dugouts. They walked through the Third's rear areas themselves in the Empire's armour, with the Empire's codes, as Styx Watch, and nobody stopped them. On the Archflamen's authority they also took over the Echo Mast. It had been dark since the Tabularium fell, since nothing the Archflamen could receive from the noosphere would help him. Hesper's signallers did not reopen it to the noosphere. They turned it to broadcast downward, on the old bands the Arsenal had used to speak with the holds below Depth 40 before there were any holds below Depth 40, and broadcast for nine cycles. The Third's relay operators heard a carrier tone on every set on the Flats, with something beneath it like a distant voice, which neither they nor the Répertoire could read. The Répertoire logged that it was being sent down the river. It was heard there. Down the Phlegethon, and the Lethe where it met the Phlegethon at Depth 43, and the Cocytus where it ran under the ice, the things that had been waiting in the deep for a war heard the Echo Mast calling them to the Pyriphlegethon Arsenal, with the promise of slaughter and salvage at the end of it, and began to come up.

### 4.4 The Attack on Leaf 52-33 (Rotation Six, Cycle 19)

The first the Third knew of the Forsworn came on the nineteenth cycle of Rotation Six, when the Forty-Fourth Trench Regiment made an attack with Hoplon carriers on the second belt on Leaf 52-33. It was the best-prepared attack the Third had made on the second belt since the Saturation. The Forty-Fourth had planned it for a month. Thirty-one Hoplons went in under a short, heavy barrage at the second bell, in a wedge, timed so that the barrage lifted as they reached the wire. They crossed the wire and the forward trench on their tracks and dropped their ramps on the support trench behind it, putting fifteen hundred breachers into the second belt in four minutes. By the end of the first hour Centurion Marrow's Sixth Cohort held four hundred metres of the support trench, the Forty-Fourth's cohorts were crossing no-man's land to widen the breach, and the regiment's colonel was asking the Fifth Line Phalanx for the Twentieth Armoured.

The Archflamen's counter-attack came along the support trench from the east. Its soldiers wore the Empire's grey armour and the cloaks of the Styx Watch, and their transponders showed on the Third's visors as friendly. They moved with a speed and assurance the Third had not met in nine years on the Flats, ignored cover, and carried short black swords, which they used in the fire bays. The Third's rounds did not penetrate their armour, while theirs went through the Third's. By the end of the second hour the breach was closed. All thirty-one Hoplons were burning inside the second belt, the Forty-Fourth's cohorts in no-man's land had been stopped by the counter-barrage, and of the fifteen hundred breachers in the support trench only sixty, Marrow among them, returned over the wire after dark. Marrow's report to her colonel stated that the counter-attack had worn the Empire's armour and shown the Empire's codes, and that soldiers struck down by its black swords had not uploaded. The report passed from the Fifth Line Phalanx to Quarterdeck Nineteen and from there to the Répertoire, which identified the codes as those of the Ninth Styx Legion cohort logged at the Mora Cut eighteen cycles earlier. Four hundred and nine of the fifteen hundred breachers had been killed by the black swords. All four hundred and nine were null.

The Command at Watchpoint Hades read Marrow's report on the forty-first cycle of Rotation Six, with the picket's log from the Mora Cut, the Répertoire's record of the carrier tone and a note from the technical officers on the four hundred and nine nulls. The technical officers had not seen a weapon do this before, but knew of one in the archive, and referred the Command to the Ninth Styx Legion's own records of what had happened eighteen hundred years before to soldiers of the Ninth cut by Styx-iron at the confluence. The Ninth's records confirmed that the cohort passed through at the Mora Cut had been lost in Rotation Two, and that its centurion's code had been reissued from an older one first issued eleven centuries before to a Legate named Hesper Calloway, who had refused the Command's order at the confluence, gone into the Styx with nine cohorts of the Legion, and been entered as null. The Ninth's records called them the Forsworn.

Castorre's first understanding was that the Archflamen had obtained the Ninth Styx Legion's codes. His chief of staff, Leda Corvis, corrected him. The Archflamen had obtained the Ninth Styx Legion's dead, soldiers who had gone into the river and come back, and who knew the Third's codes because they had once been the Empire's own. Four hundred of them had closed a breach held by fifteen hundred. They carried swords whose wounds the vats could not undo, brought six hundred years of trench war with them, and held the Echo Mast. Castorre reported the substance to the Command the same cycle, renewed his request for the new Line Phalanx and asked for something to be done about the Mast.

### 4.5 The Command's Response

The Command's response was slow and divided. The discovery that the Archflamen had allies from the river transformed the siege from an internal matter of the Tartarean theatre into something the Empire's wider institutions could no longer ignore, and each of them reacted according to its own interests. The Répertoire revised its estimate of the inner belt and the Acropolis upward by a third and asked for the Echo Mast to be destroyed as a matter of priority, though it could not say how this was to be done while the Mast stood inside the lance envelope and behind three belts of trenches. The Pontifical College, for whom an Archflamen allied to oath-breakers from the Styx was a far greater scandal than an Archflamen in revolt, pressed for the Third to be reinforced at once. The Ninth Styx Legion demanded the right to deal with its own dead and was refused, since the Command could not spare it from the confluence. The Directorate's clerks resumed their search for three Trench Regiments. For the soldiers of the Third the consequence was simpler. From the forty-first cycle of Rotation Six, every regiment on the Flats was ordered to challenge any party of Styx Watch on sight, regardless of its codes, and to fire upon any party that did not halt. In the remaining months of the rotation, the Third killed eleven of its own soldiers in the rear areas under that order and no Forsworn.

The Third's general situation in Rotation Six worsened accordingly. Its attacks on the second belt failed more often and more expensively than before, its rear areas could no longer be considered safe, and its null rate rose for the fourth rotation in succession. Castorre was criticised in the Command for his conduct of the campaign and defended by the College, and his position was weaker at the end of the rotation than at its start. His plan for the second belt had been to keep pressing until it broke somewhere. By the end of Rotation Six it was the Third that was being pressed.

### 4.6 The Cohort of Nemesis (Rotation Seven, Cycle 30)

At the start of Rotation Seven, Castorre's requests were met, after a fashion. On the thirtieth cycle a convoy of the Tartarus Fleet came down to Landing Shelf Kappa carrying the [[Eighth Line Phalanx]], made up of the Fiftieth, [[Fifty-Second Trench Regiment|Fifty-Second]] and [[Fifty-Fourth Trench Regiment|Fifty-Fourth]] Trench Regiments. They had been designated for a reformation peak further up the Phlegethon, and Castorre's family in the Pontifical College had secured their diversion. The Fifty-Fourth was a regiment of Lethe Hold drafts raised from the ration rolls in Rotation Two, when the shells stopped coming, and most of its soldiers had never been to Depth 40.

The Command had also acted without consulting Castorre. The Répertoire would not allow the Archflamen's war to spread down the rivers, and the carrier tone of the Echo Mast had been heard at Depth 43 and below, where things the Third had never been built to fight were now moving toward the Arsenal. The Pyrrhus Command, which commanded the Empire's pure-blood cohorts in the theatre, had sent envoys under its own seal to the Olympian orders of the ceiling worlds to seek their aid. The first to answer was [[Cohors Nemesis Prima]]. The Cohort of [[Nemesis]] was a pure-blood order of the children of Nemesis and their descendants, recruited at the cohort-house on [[Rhamnous Primaris]] from those with a blood index above ninety. It did not hold ground and had never held a line in its history. It went where an oath had been broken and made the breaking cost, and for that purpose kept a battle-carrier and two strike cruisers on permanent readiness at Rhamnous, which allowed it to answer within a month. It sent half its strength, two thousand and forty of its sworn, under the Prefect of the Cohort himself, Lucan [[Prefect Ardent|Ardent]]. The battle-carrier [[Inexorable]] came down through the ceiling over the southern Flats on the thirtieth cycle with the strike cruisers Adrasteia and Rhamnous and nine escorts, and held station beyond the reach of the ceiling-lances.

Prefect Ardent sent no signal to the Third. He set about the mission he had assigned himself, the capture and destruction of the [[Phlegethon Docks]], which would cut the Arsenal off from the river. He required no assistance from the Third and saw no reason to involve it. While the Third's staff on Quarterdeck Nineteen were adjusting their plans to fit the Eighth Line Phalanx into the line, Ardent and his centurion-masters were bringing the Cohort's strength down onto the ash.

The Cohort's conduct was characteristic of the Olympian orders and was resented accordingly. The pure-blood orders of the ceiling worlds answered to their own houses and to the Pyrrhus Command's seal, and none of them had ever acknowledged the authority of a Lord-General of a Siege Army in the field. They regarded the mortal and legacy armies of the Tartarean theatre as an instrument for holding ground, which was a task the orders did not perform, and Ardent saw no more reason to place the Cohort under Castorre than Castorre would have seen to place the Third under a Directorate quartermaster. The Third's staff learned of the Cohort's landing from the Tartarus Fleet's movement logs. Castorre protested to the Command, which forwarded the protest to the Pyrrhus Command, which did not reply. It was the first of several occasions in the siege on which an order of the Empire fought the Arsenal beside the Third without reference to it, and in each case the Third's commanders found that the results were of more use to them than the arrangement deserved.

The Cohort itself was organised for exactly the kind of operation Ardent intended. Its two thousand and forty sworn were divided into ten centuries of about two hundred, each under a centurion-master, with heavy sections in Nemesis plate for the assault and line sections for the work behind it. Its vehicles were few and fast, chosen to be carried by its [[Lantern Gunship|Lantern gunships]]. It brought eleven heavy carriers, two squadrons of assault tanks, a number of Hoplons and siege carriages, a company of Furies and a troop of Areion striders for reconnaissance. It brought no trench equipment and few supplies, since it did not expect to be on the ash for more than a month, and its sworn carried implants and uploaded to the Cohort's own vats aboard the Inexorable, which spared them Watchpoint Hades.

### 4.7 The Prefect's Plan

The Phlegethon Docks lay at the south-eastern foot of the spur, where the Arsenal met the river. They were the Arsenal's river port, eleven kilometres of quays, slipways and warehouses where the ore barges came up from the lower shelves and the shell barges went out to the holds downriver. They lay beyond the Demus Gorge from the Flats, out of reach of the Third's line, and the Third had never fought there. Three chain-bridges carried the docks' roads across the Gorge to the spur. The Cohort's Lantern gunships came down through the ceiling cracks south of the lance envelope in a long loop and landed the Cohort in a few hours on the Gravel Strand, a shelf of river shingle at the foot of the Kerberos Hills forty kilometres south of the docks.

Ardent chose the docks as his objective for three reasons. They were the Arsenal's only access to the river, and whatever came up the Phlegethon to help the Archflamen would come through them. An attack on the docks would also force the Archflamen to commit troops to a new front. The docks had never been threatened by the Third, and their garrison had been stripped to man the second belt, so that if the Archflamen wished to keep them he would have to bring troops back from the second belt, which the Third would then find thinner. The third reason the Cohort kept to itself, as it kept most things. There were Forsworn on the Flats. The Cohort of Nemesis had been searching for Hesper Calloway for eleven hundred years. A Legate who had broken her oath to the Command and then sworn on the Styx that she had not was the oldest debt on the Cohort's books, and Ardent had brought his Castigator, [[Ennio Dask]], whose work for the Cohort was the questioning of oath-breakers, to extract answers from any of the Forsworn the Cohort could take alive.

Ardent's plan was carefully considered. He would launch a fast armoured column up the Strand along the riverbank, envelop the docks and strike wherever the defenders proved weakest. He could not afford a war of attrition, and since his ships would not risk the ceiling-lances, he would be committing his sworn to street fighting in the docks without fire support from above. To seal off the battle from reinforcement, he would capture the three chain-bridges across the Demus Gorge and drop them into it, after which nothing could cross from the spur or the Flats to the docks. That task went to the Cohort's Third Century under Centurion-Master [[Ilario Kett]], who would afterwards join the attack on the docks.

The arrival of the Cohort changed the strategic situation more than its numbers suggested. Two thousand pure-bloods in the Arsenal's rear could strike anywhere, flying over the second belt to land on the spur itself. Hesper understood this, and so did the Archflamen's commanders. They could not risk the Cohort descending on the Acropolis with its garrison absent, and to guard against it they had to withdraw troops from the second belt to cover the Acropolis, the docks, the vaults and the lance silos. The second belt lost thirty thousand soldiers in a week. Two thousand of the Cohort of Nemesis had moved more of the Archflamen's army than the Third's general attack of Rotation Four.

### 4.8 The Battle of the Phlegethon Docks (Rotation Seven, Cycle 34)

The Cohort began its advance on the docks on the thirty-fourth cycle of Rotation Seven. Its Areion striders, fast bipedal mounts ridden by the Cohort's scouts, went ahead of the column along the shingle in search of blocking positions, in constant contact with the Lantern gunships flying cover overhead. Behind them came the Cohort's heavy carriers and assault tanks, its Hoplons and siege carriages, and the mobile mortar crawlers it called Furies, which fired keyed shells on the move. The Third Century remained at the landing ground until the bridges were wanted. The Arsenal responded with its own scouts, light crawlers and walker pickets of the dock garrison, some of which were caught and destroyed by the striders. The Archflamen's commanders soon knew where the Cohort was going, and reinforcements were sent from the spur to the docks. Ardent led the column himself in his command carrier, the Debt Unpaid, and by the end of the first watch it was in sight of the docks.

As Ardent closed on the docks, Kett's Century went for the bridges. The southern chain-bridge was the longest of the three, a kilometre and a half of iron deck hung on chains above a gorge nine kilometres deep. Kett's assault sections jumped from their Lanterns at low level onto the eastern end of the deck and took the Arsenal's checkpoint there before its garrison could reach its guns. Most of the garrison fled, and those who stood were cut down. Two more Lanterns raked the checkpoint and set the far end of the deck burning, and Kett's line sections, landing as the gunships touched down, drove the remainder of the bridge garrison back into the assault sections and destroyed it between them. The bridge was taken in eleven minutes. Kett placed a heavy weapons section on the western end to cover the road from the spur, and soon afterwards an Arsenal column of Thureos tanks, Hoplon carriers, walkers and a battery of divine mortars on crawlers came along it toward the docks. The heavy weapons section knocked out the lead tank with a lance-cannon and destroyed the vehicles behind it as they tried to pass, and within minutes the road was blocked by burning hulls. While it held, the assault sections set charges on the anchor towers and chains. Kett withdrew his Century by Lantern at a cost of three wounded, all fit to continue, and dropped the bridge into the Gorge. By the end of the cycle the Third Century had taken and dropped the other two bridges as well, and the Arsenal's roads to the docks had been cut.

Ardent's striders, meanwhile, had found an Arsenal armoured column moving south out of the docks to meet him. He sent his Lanterns against it and his column forward, and by the time his heavy carriers came up the shingle was covered in the smoke of burning hulls. The carriers cut through the rest of the column as it tried to regain the docks, and Ardent swung east around them. With Kett's report of the first bridge in hand, he planned to attack the docks from the east, draw the garrison onto himself and then put Kett's Century in from the west to crush it between them. Guided by the striders, his siege carriages demolished the warehouses the garrison had fortified before the sworn left their carriers, and the sworn cleared the rubble behind them. The Lanterns returned from the ships rearmed and bombed the garrison wherever the striders found it, and the Furies fired salvo after salvo into the docks, moving after each one so that the Arsenal's gunners on the spur, firing back across the Gorge, could not find them. Ardent did not halt at the dark watch, and by the first bell of the second cycle the Cohort had cleared the eastern approaches to the northern slipways and the garrison had fallen back. Kett's Century landed west of the main slipways during the night as a stop against its escape.

By then the garrison held the large buildings around the slipways, the shell sheds, barge hangars and repair shops, and had turned them into strongpoints. On the second cycle Ardent was content to shell and probe them. He sought the destruction of the garrison along with the docks, and gave it a cycle to bring more of its strength into the ring. On the third cycle he gave the Cohort's Sixth Century to Castigator Dask, with the honour of carrying the Wheel, the Cohort's war-standard, into battle in the hands of Signifer Thessaly Kord. The Sixth hooked south around the slipways to seal the ring, supported by siege carriages and Furies, and was soon engaged in street fighting in the ruins of the shell sheds, where the Cohort's sworn fought through the rubble with flame, sword and keyed grenade. At the end of the cycle Dask reported fifteen of the Sixth transferred and the southern docks clear. On the fourth cycle Ardent's centurion-masters agreed a plan of three concentric attacks on the slipways, from the east by Ardent, then from the south by Dask, then from the west by Kett, each in turn tightening the ring over three cycles. During Kett's attack the Third Century overran the northern barge pens and found Forsworn there. Ardent ordered that as many as possible be taken alive. It was what the Cohort had come for.

On the tenth cycle Ardent ordered the final assault and led it himself on foot, with the First, Second and Fifth Centuries, into the ruins of the slipways where the last of the dock garrison was holding out. The garrison broke before him. It had been bait. The Forsworn had waited in the dry docks and drainage culverts beneath the slipways, and they emerged in the middle of the Cohort's assault. Signifer Kord was killed in the first minute and the Wheel fell with her. The standard could not be left on the ground, and Ardent crossed sixty metres of open slipway under fire to reach it as the Forsworn did. The Cohort's sworn and the Forsworn fought over Kord's body without either giving ground, and while the rest of the Cohort fought its way out of the trap, Ardent killed the last of the Forsworn around the standard and raised it. Hesper then joined the fight herself, in the black parade armour of the Ninth Styx Legion, still wet with Styx-water. She cut down the first of the Cohort's sworn to reach her and engaged Ardent, who met her with the Wheel in one hand and his sword, the Scale, in the other. Ardent was a son of Nemesis with a blood index of ninety-seven and three hundred years of service with the Cohort. He was outmatched. Hesper had held the confluence of the Styx for six hundred years and had been in the river for eleven hundred more. She drove him back across the slipway until his armour split, knocked him down and struck the Scale from his hand, and was raising her sword over him when a heavy gun hit her in the side. Castigator Dask had arrived with a section of the Cohort's heavy sworn. He struck Hesper down with a blow from his rod of office that split her breastplate, and turned to see to the Prefect.
