The sappers sealed and guarded every exit, then reopened the heat-well conduits that ran beneath the vault from the Arsenal's casting lines, which the Archflamen's engineers had shut off at the start of the siege, and let the Phlegethon in. The river's fire filled the vault to its roof and set there, entombing the defenders and the Ninefold Brand with them, if the river's fire could kill them. The sappers regarded it as their revenge.

The second battle for Vault 9 was fought on the two hundred and tenth cycle of Rotation Eleven, after the sappers had driven three new approach galleries beneath Leaves 55 and 56 so that the loss of one could not decide the outcome. The breaching charges were smaller and placed under the vault's outer storerooms, where the defenders were fewest, and the assault companies went in with heavy flamers keyed by children of Hephaestus, whose fire the Cinderborn could not simply walk through. The sappers took the outer third of the vault in a cycle and held it for six against counter-attacks from the inner passages, until the Frost Masons flooded two of the three galleries with seep they had been storing behind ice for a rotation, and the companies in the vault were withdrawn before the third could be cut. The third battle, on the three hundred and sixtieth cycle, was fought as a diversion for the Twenty-Second's assault on the surface. The sappers broke into the vault from below as the Twenty-Second's breachers came down the shafts from above, and the defenders, caught between them, were driven into the inner storerooms, where the sappers sealed them in.

### 8.8 The Great Mine (Rotation Eleven, Cycle 80 to Rotation Twelve, Cycle 230)

The failure at Vault 9 did not halt the war beneath elsewhere. Every regiment now had sapper companies working beneath it, and a second front ran under the length of the Flats, where the silent fighting continued every cycle and took its toll of both sides. The Hephaestean sappers worked steadily toward the objective they had been sent for, which was the Cinder Wall. The Wall was thirty metres high and twelve thick, built of the Arsenal's cast basalt with bunkers and barracks within it and guns along its top, and over the rotations of the siege the Frost Masons had laid trenches and pillboxes before it, so that it now formed one further line of works, thinner than the three belts and still requiring assault. Kastellan's plan had always been to mine the Wall and blow breaches through it. Phaethon fire and the Wrath bombers might achieve the same result in time, but they were needed elsewhere, and until the Third's trenches were close enough to engage the Wall over open sights, a great mine offered the best chance of a breach.

On the hundred and ninetieth cycle of Rotation Twelve, more than a rotation after the mining had begun, the Sixty-First Sapper Company reported that it had reached the Wall's deep footings beneath [[Leaf 57-44]] and was ready to lay a mine. The explosive was carried down to the chamber in complete secrecy and stacked by hand over many cycles of silent work, with a second detonator wired to the first. On the surface, the [[Thirty-Fifth Trench Regiment]] prepared to go first through the breach and force a way through the last works between the Third and the Acropolis. To draw the Archflamen's attention from Leaf 57-44, mining elsewhere was stepped up, and beneath Leaf 56-46 the sappers deliberately drove heavy borers at the Wall as a diversion. The Frost Masons heard them at once and sent several counter-mines against them, and there was more bloody fighting in the dark. The mine under Leaf 57-44 would be the largest of the siege, and it nearly emptied the Seventh Line Phalanx's stores. The Third would have a single attempt.

The diversion beneath Leaf 56-46 did its work at heavy cost. Over forty cycles the Frost Masons sent eleven counter-mines against the borers there, and the sapper company that manned them lost four hundred names to the Deep List, more than two thirds of its strength. The borers reached within ninety metres of the Wall before the last of them was buried, and by then the Frost Masons had committed most of their strength on that stretch of the front to stopping them. Beneath Leaf 57-44, eight hundred metres to the north, the Sixty-First Sapper Company finished laying its mine without a single counter-mine being driven against it.

The decision to mine the Wall at a single point was Kastellan's own and was opposed by the Forge's Sapper-Prefects, who preferred a series of smaller mines along the Wall's length in the manner that had succeeded in the north. Kastellan judged that the Frost Masons would find most of a series long before it was ready, and that one mine dug in silence, behind a diversion loud enough to hold their attention, had a better chance of reaching the footings undetected. The Sapper-Prefects deferred to him and recorded their objection with the Forge.

The charge itself amounted to nineteen hundred tonnes of Hephaestean blasting compound, carried down the incline behind Leaf 57-44 in packs of twenty kilograms by sappers working in silence, over one hundred and twelve cycles. It filled a chamber cut beneath the Wall's footings eighty-six metres down and was tamped with spoil and sandbags along three hundred metres of gallery behind it to drive its force upward. It was the largest single charge the Forge's sappers had ever laid, and they doubted that the Wall's footings, cast by the Arsenal's founders to stand on the shelf for ten thousand years, would yield to anything smaller.

Zero hour was set for the two hundred and thirtieth cycle of Rotation Twelve. Every sapper was withdrawn from the galleries, and the mine was fired by a one-handed son of Hephaestus, a sapper-major who had lost the other hand in the first rotation of the war beneath. A column of fire, smoke and rock rose hundreds of metres from the Flats in front of the Wall, spread beneath the ceiling and fell back, and the shock rolled across no-man's land through the trenches of the Thirty-Fifth. When the smoke cleared, a long stretch of the Wall was gone, replaced by a crater one hundred and ninety metres across. The Third had its breach. The Thirty-Fifth's guns began at once to fire upon the ground beyond, to prepare it for the first wave in its Hoplons.

By the time the [[Great Mine]] was fired, the Hephaestean sappers and their trench volunteers had driven more than three hundred kilometres of galleries beneath the Cinder Flats, fired two hundred and seventy mines and some four thousand darts, and met the Archflamen's counter-mines in more than nine hundred recorded actions. The Deep List then held one hundred and forty thousand names, among them two Sapper-Prefects and the colonels of three sapper regiments. The Archflamen's losses below ground could not be counted, since his miners carried no implants and the dead of both sides lay where the galleries had buried them. The sappers estimated that they had killed three of the enemy for every one of their own, and that the Frost Masons had killed four sappers for every one of theirs. Both figures were guesses. The war beneath had won the Third ground on the northern leaves and a breach in the Cinder Wall, and it had cost the Third a class of loss it had never before had to count, which the vats at Watchpoint Hades could not repair.

### 8.9 The War Above (Rotation Ten, Cycle 250 to Rotation Twelve, Cycle 230)

With the Third refitted and Behemoths of its own on the Flats, the balance had shifted back toward the Empire on the ground. The arrival of the Wrath wings allowed it to contest the ceiling geometry as well. With the drowned had come the things that flew. The Stymphalian swarms of the deep had always come up the rivers, and for six hundred years the ceiling-lances had burned them out of the dark as fast as they appeared. Now the lances belonged to the Archflamen and the swarms rose unopposed, accompanied by the drowned ships' own craft, old [[Lantern Gunship|Lanterns]] and attack boats flown by drowned crews, which struck where they liked. The Third had almost nothing to fire back with. Its plan had never anticipated a need for anti-air guns, since the lances had kept the air clear for six centuries, and the few it had brought had long since been turned against ground targets or lost to shellfire. Swarms and Lanterns struck supply dumps and gun lines across the Flats and twice cut the cables at the foot of the Cinder Stair, halting supply. The Third's losses from the air grew every cycle, and until the Wraths arrived the air over the Flats belonged to the drowned.

Until the Wraths arrived, Kastellan's staff improvised what air defence they could. Divine batteries keyed by children of Artemis were found to burst their lunar shards effectively among the swarms when fused for air bursts, and four batteries were withdrawn from the gun line and sited around the cable heads at the foot of the Stair and the main shell dumps. The Forge sent forty pairs of old Hephaestean [[Flak|flak]] guns from its stores, and the Third built towers for them on the old outer belt. None of this was sufficient to contest the air over the Flats, but it reduced the losses at the Stair, and after the eightieth cycle of Rotation Ten the cables were not cut again.

The Tartarus Fleet's commitment gave Kastellan the means to answer. The interceptor wing of ten squadrons, about twenty Wraths in each, would defend the Flats with patrols every watch and escort the bombers. The bomber wing of seven squadrons of a dozen heavy Wraths each, with four Vigil-pattern craft for reconnaissance and command, could pound the Archflamen every watch, bomb strongpoints at low level and carpet whole stretches of the front before an attack, and it gave the Third a reach well beyond its guns. Preparing the craft after their journey, stockpiling fuel and munitions and building landing fields on the old outer belt took time. The first missions were flown on the three hundred and thirtieth cycle of Rotation Ten, and the Flats, which had never had a sky, heard engines in the dark overhead for the first time in six hundred years.

To the trench soldiers, the Wrath crews seemed to have the easiest war on the Flats, living in hardened barracks far back on the old outer belt and spared both the shelling and the walk into the guns. The crews themselves knew otherwise. The Arsenal had built its own air defence over eight thousand years against the swarms, and on top of its guns stood the ceiling-lances, which the Archflamen's gunners had learned to fire flat across the Flats and could fire upward just as well. The crews called the Acropolis the Wasp Nest. Every raid upon it met a wall of flak and lance fire, and the swarms rose out of the river cloud to meet the bombers with no regard for their own losses. Over the Acropolis a Wrath crewman had less chance of returning than a trench soldier, and when he did not return he was more likely to be null, for the [[Noosphere|noosphere]] over the cone lay nearest the Echo Mast. The relay crews at the Wrath fields found that uploads from crews lost over the cone arrived at Watchpoint Hades empty half the time. The war in the air was as bitter as the war on the ground.

The Archflamen's gunners did not confine the lances to the defence of the cone. In the first rotation of the air war they fired them flat across the Flats at the Wrath fields on the old outer belt, which lay within their reach, and destroyed thirty craft on the ground in three nights before the fields were moved back beyond the lance envelope and their aircraft sheltered in revetments of basalt. The move added forty minutes to every sortie over the cone and reduced the number the wings could fly in a watch. The Tenth Ordnance Phalanx was ordered to give the lances priority in its counter-battery fire, and over the next two rotations it destroyed three of the lances in their silos about the spur.

The Acropolis was the bombers' principal target. The first Vigil missions found that the Archflamen had not lived solely on the stores of his vaults. A shanty-town of foundries, machine shops and casting pits had grown up around the foot of the cone inside the Cinder Wall, repairing tanks and making shells and rifles every watch to supply the front, and these forges, beyond the reach of the Third's guns, became the bombers' first targets. The Acropolis itself was too well defended beneath its wards to be attacked directly. The forges were defended almost as strongly, and the air war over them was the fiercest of the siege. Other missions were flown against strongpoints at the front and the Archflamen's roads, but the cone came first.

### 8.10 The Raids on the Forges (Rotation Eleven, Cycles 150 to 159)

The largest air operation of the siege was flown against the forges at the foot of the cone over nine cycles in the middle of Rotation Eleven. The bomber wing sent every serviceable heavy Wrath against them at every watch, escorted by the whole of the interceptor wing, while the Tenth Ordnance Phalanx shelled the flak positions on the Cinder Wall to draw their fire. By the end of the operation the Vigils counted sixty-three foundries and casting pits destroyed or burned out, and the Arsenal's output of shells to the front fell by about a third for the following rotation. The cost was severe. The bomber wing lost forty-one heavy Wraths, more than half its strength, and the interceptor wing lost fifty-eight craft in the fighting over the cone, with nearly half their crews logged null. The Fleet replaced the losses within two rotations, as it had promised, but the bomber wing did not fly against the cone in such strength again, and within a rotation the forges had been rebuilt beneath the walls of the Acropolis, where its wards protected them.

By the end of Rotation Twelve the Wrath wings had flown more than nine thousand sorties over the Flats and lost three hundred and twelve craft. They had not won the air over the Acropolis, where the lances and the swarms remained too strong, but they had won it over the rest of the Flats. The swarms no longer struck the cable heads at the Stair, the convoys reached Kappa without loss, and the Third's gun lines were no longer bombed at every watch. For the soldiers in the trenches it was the most important change in their war since the arrival of the Behemoths.

### 8.11 The Balance in the Air

The Wrath wings were directed from the Fleet's standing squadron over Kappa and remained the Fleet's own, so that Kastellan's control over them was limited to the targets he requested and the priority he gave each. The arrangement worked better than the one with the Forge's sappers, since the Fleet's air officers kept a liaison staff on Quarterdeck Nineteen and accepted most of the Third's requests, but the Fleet reserved the right to withdraw the wings to its other charges in the deep, and twice in Rotation Twelve it took a squadron of interceptors away to the Acheron stairs for a month. Kastellan protested both withdrawals to Watchpoint Hades and was told that the Flats were not the only front in Tartarus.

Kastellan was assured that the Fleet's commitment would last as long as the siege, and that a steady flow of replacement craft and crews would keep both wings at strength. The Archflamen's strength in the air was unknown and difficult to count, since the swarms came and went with the river, and estimates ranged from two hundred to five hundred. The drowned and the swarms probably held the advantage in numbers at first. As losses mounted on both sides and the Fleet's replacements arrived, most crews judged by the middle of Rotation Eleven that the two sides were about level, and from then on the Wrath wings gained, if only by numbers. The interceptor wing flew mostly the standard Wrath, the well-armed and well-armoured workhorse of the Fleet's strike wings, with a few squadrons of the lighter Wrath Seconds, which could stay with the swarms in a turning fight. The heavy Wraths of the bomber wing had long range and a large bomb load and could not fly without escort, and the Vigils carried augurs and imagers in place of bombs and were few and precious. Against them came the Stymphalians, small, fast and able to turn inside anything the Fleet flew, which came in clouds about the Wrath formations, and the drowned Lanterns, heavier fighter-bombers that could still hold their own against a Wrath. Nothing larger had yet come up out of the river to fly. The crews on the Wrath fields discussed what would follow if something did.

## 9 Into the Breach

> *"Every one of them that does not come back brings the Imperator nearer. There is nothing in the Empire that can stop what we began to call down so many rotations ago. Soon it will come up at our asking."*
>
> Haruspex Ilan Dace, entered under CODE 31 by the Conclave at Watchpoint Hades, Rotation Twelve

### 9.1 The Hoplon Assault (Leaf 57-44, Rotation Twelve, Cycle 230)

The assault on the crater was to be led by [[Hoplon Carrier|Hoplon]] carriers. Two cohorts of the Thirty-Fifth Trench Regiment would ride to the breach in thirty of them, supported by [[Thureos Tank|Thureos]] tanks and a company of Colossi, and at the breach the Hoplons would drop their ramps and put eight hundred breachers onto the rubble at once, enough to overwhelm whatever the mine had left alive. Behind them would come more of the Thirty-Fifth on foot and the shock breachers of the regiment's grenadier cohort in Kentauros carriers. The [[Phaethon Heavy Mortar|Phaethons]] of the [[Tenth Ordnance Phalanx]] were already boxing the breach off from reinforcement, and the Wrath bombers would come in low against anything moving up behind the Wall.

The [[Cinder Wall]] was the last and oldest of the [[Sabinus Orrhae|Archflamen]]'s rings. It encircled the foot of the [[Acropolis (Pyriphlegethon)|Acropolis]] in a single unbroken line twenty-two kilometres long, cast in six courses of basalt by the Arsenal's first founders, thirty metres thick at the footing and capped by a parapet of the same stone. More than a hundred towers stood along it at intervals of two hundred metres, each with slits for heavy guns and plunging fire onto the ground below, and inside it ran bunkers, magazines, shelters and long corridors through which the garrison could move from one end to the other under cover. Along the parapet sat the old automatic guns the founders had built against the swarms. In front of it the Archflamen had dug further trenches, pillboxes, wire, minefields and tank ditches. The Wall had one weakness, which was that it had no depth. Behind it lay only the forges and the cone, and once a long enough stretch was down, the [[Tartarus Front Soldiers|Third]] could pour through the gap faster than the Archflamen could close it.

The Hoplons moved off before the dust of the Great Mine had settled, crossing the cratered ground toward the breach in a ragged line, each with fifty soldiers of the Thirty-Fifth packed inside. Most of the garrison on that stretch of the Wall was still dazed by the mine, but the survivors manned their pillboxes and fire trenches, and the Archflamen's counter-fire came down among the carriers in growing weight, beginning with mortars and rising through [[Divine Material Ammunition|divine mortar]] rounds to the Arsenal's own siege guns firing from their pits around the cone. One Hoplon took a shell directly into its open troop bay, and the relay at the Thirty-Fifth's command bunker logged forty-four uploads from it within two seconds, six of them [[Null Transfer|null]]. Lance-cannon on the Wall engaged the first wave and were largely defeated by the Hoplons' heavy front plates, which had been built for exactly that purpose, while the Thureos tanks fired back at the parapet. A second carrier burned, a third lost a track and a fourth exploded.

At the breach the ramps came down and the Thirty-Fifth went out into the fire. The heavy guns on the Wall caught many of them in grazing fire before they had cleared the ramps, and whole sections went down together in the ash. At the lip of the crater the regiment found that the mine had thrown up a rampart of its own, a ring of broken blocks of the Wall and its footings, some the size of a Hoplon, which gave the breachers cover and stopped the carriers and tanks completely. Their task done, the Hoplons backed out with their guns firing over the breachers' heads, and from the rim onward the fight belonged to the infantry, who went forward through the rubble under plunging fire from the towers on either side of the gap. Many officers fell in the first minutes. Centurion [[Abel Strand]] of the Nineteenth Cohort lost a leg to a mortar round among the blocks and lay behind them for nine hours, alive and therefore unable to upload. [[Edrin Solk|Provost Edrin Solk]] of the regiment's provost section took his cohort forward over the rim, across the crater floor and up the far slope between the broken ends of the Wall, which the Thureos tanks had swept clear of defenders. His party had nearly reached the top when the Archflamen counter-attacked.

The Archflamen's commanders had anticipated the assault and drawn in every soldier they could reach from the neighbouring stretches of the Wall. Waiting in the rubble at the top of the crater were the [[Laistrygonian Half-Bloods|Laistrygons]], dosed with furnace-draught, the heat-well stimulant the Arsenal's overseers had once used to extract a double shift from them, and held back until the last moment. They came down the slope at a run onto the Thirty-Fifth as it climbed, and a regiment caught on a slope by enemies twice its height had no chance against them. Solk was among those killed, cut in half by a casting-line rock borer, though his upload was clean and he woke at [[Watchpoint Hades]] nineteen cycles later. The Laistrygons carried their charge into the bottom of the crater, where the fighting became a butchery in which one of them fell for every dozen soldiers it killed. The Archflamen's gunners then shelled the crater itself, killing friend and enemy without distinction, and the relay at the command bunker filled with uploads faster than its crews could count them.

The Thirty-Fifth's tanks could not cross the rubble, and without them the breachers could not fight their way up the slope against defenders who fought as though the Wall would fall upon them if they gave a metre. The second wave of Kentauros carriers and foot centuries fared no better, and the fighting continued into the dark watch. Time after time the breachers went over the rim, and time after time the Archflamen moved guns to sweep the crater or counter-charged and drove them out. In the dark watch the Thirty-Fifth withdrew to its own trenches. Of the two cohorts that had gone in first, most of the soldiers had been transferred, and some centuries had been lost entirely. The dead lay three and four deep in places, and the wrecks of tanks and Hoplons lay across the sector. When the firing died down, the reclaimers of the [[Répertoire du Savoir Athénien|Répertoire]] went over the ground with the Quiet Ones behind them, cutting the implants out of the dead who had not uploaded so that the Répertoire could check and log them. The regiment's command acknowledged that the first attempt had failed. [[Vidar Osk|Colonel Vidar Osk]] asked for a second, which he would prepare properly and lead in person.

The first assault on the breach had cost the Thirty-Fifth eleven thousand [[Transfer (UOE)|transfers]] in a single cycle, nine hundred of them null, and nineteen of its thirty Hoplons. The figure was modest by the standards of [[Cinder Flats|the Flats]]. The proportion alarmed the Répertoire, since almost every soldier of the two leading cohorts had been lost, and a regiment could not lose its leading cohorts in that manner more than a few times before it ceased to exist as a fighting formation. Kastellan approved Osk's request on the condition that the second attempt be planned with his own staff and supported by the whole of the Tenth Ordnance Phalanx.

### 9.2 The Second Assault (Rotation Twelve, Cycle 246)

The second assault went in on the two hundred and forty-sixth cycle of [[Imperial Calendar|Rotation]] Twelve, planned with considerably more care. The staff had concluded that the first attack had failed on the rubble, which no armour could cross, and for fourteen cycles the Phaethon batteries of the Tenth Ordnance Phalanx, firing from their pits south of the [[Demus Gorge]], shelled the breach itself every watch with their heaviest rounds to break the blocks small enough for tanks to pass. The rest of the Tenth shelled the Wall and its towers on either side of the gap to wreck the parapet and the old automatic guns. The bombardment consumed a large share of the Seventh Line Phalanx's shells. The staff had also concluded that the first attack had been too narrow. In it, the Archflamen had held the breach by bringing soldiers along the Wall's inner corridors from the stretches on either side, out of reach of the shelling. The second attack would therefore go in along the whole of the regiment's front, so that those soldiers would be pinned by attacks on their own stretches, and the Colossi would accompany the flanking attacks to blow new breaches with their guns. With several breaches to hold, the Archflamen's strength would be dispersed and less able to meet the main blow.

The Archflamen was better prepared as well. His gunners had had two weeks to range the ground, and the Hoplons met a storm of lance fire and missiles on the way in, many of them joining the wrecks of the first attack in no-man's land. Colonel Osk led his soldiers into the crater and found it held again in strength. He was killed there in hand-to-hand fighting among the blocks, together with his signifer, by a Laistrygon's hammer, and was logged null, and his body was never found. To the north and south the wider attack had some success, and the Colossi blew two smaller breaches in the Wall, but neither could be taken and held. One of them changed hands eight times in a single cycle before the Thirty-Fifth was forced out of it. The second assault had been beaten back like the first.

### 9.3 The Meat-Grinder (Rotation Twelve, Cycle 300 to Rotation Thirteen, Cycle 340)

For the third assault, Kastellan brought the [[Ninth Assault Phalanx]] into the line to lead the attack on the Wall. Once again the Third went up into the breaches in waves, and once again it was thrown back by an Archflamen who was now fighting with everything he had left. Over the following rotation there were four more assaults on Leaf 57-44, each ending in a bloody retreat. The [[Titan (Mech)|Behemoths]] of Talos joined the fighting, and the drowned Behemoths came across the Flats to meet them, and the ground around the crater became the bloodiest of the whole siege. Both armies fed regiments into it, and it consumed them. The fourth of these assaults ended when the [[Frost Masons]] fired a mine of their own beneath the original crater, which they had been digging in silence since the second assault at the deliberate Cocytus pace the Hephaestean sappers had learned to dread. It went off beneath eleven centuries of the Ninth Assault Phalanx on the crater floor, and many of them were buried so deep in the rock that their implants never found a relay. Nine hundred names went onto the Deep List in a single second. The crater was deeper and wider when the dust settled and more choked with rubble than ever.

The Archflamen's losses in the crater were also heavy. The Répertoire estimated that he had fed one hundred and seventy thousand soldiers into the defence of the breach over the rotation, among them most of the Laistrygons still alive in the Arsenal, and that his reaction forces behind the Wall had lost more than two hundred tanks. His losses were final, as the Third's were not, and Kastellan's staff took what comfort they could from the arithmetic. It was thin comfort. The Archflamen's soldiers were being replaced from sources the Répertoire could not count, and the Third's were coming back from the vats slower with every return.

The Ninth Assault Phalanx had come down to the Flats in Rotation Eleven with the convoys that followed Kastellan's relief, an armoured formation raised on [[Callistus]] of four regiments, with two hundred and forty Thureos tanks, thirty Colossi and a regiment of breachers in Hoplons. It had been held in reserve behind the Seventh Line Phalanx for a rotation against the day the Wall was breached, intended for the exploitation of a breach once it had been won. Kastellan now committed it to winning the breach, a task for which it had not been raised, and the third assault and the four that followed it cost the Ninth and the regiments fed in beside it some six hundred thousand transfers and reduced it to less than half its strength in tanks.

Kastellan had found no way to take the breach and feared that it was consuming the army. He asked the Forge for more Behemoths, and Cradle-Lord Varne passed on the Forge's refusal, since Talos had already lost eleven engines on the Flats and would not risk the rest in a crater. The fighting at Leaf 57-44 was the fiercest on the Flats, but the whole of the Flats remained one battlefield, with attacks and counter-attacks, raids and counter-raids along the entire front every watch. The [[Forsworn]] kept up their attacks. The Unremembered raided far behind the Third's lines, coming out of the mist off the [[Lethe Cut]] to destroy gun pits and shell dumps before vanishing back into it. The First Line Phalanx on the northern front was wholly occupied by the Grey Seventh shelling, which still came over in every Arsenal barrage and still sent soldiers back to Watchpoint Hades blank or not at all. The drowned Behemoths remained on the Flats to add their weight to any major attack, and the Talos engines had to go wherever they appeared, until Varne's cohort was dispersed across the Flats in twos and threes. Reports came up to [[Quarterdeck Nineteen]] faster than Kastellan's staff could read them.

The war beneath could not supply another breach either. The Frost Masons had learned from the Great Mine and were counter-mining better than ever, and beneath the ground the deadlock was as complete as above it, with the two sides so evenly matched that every blow was met and neither had the strength to finish the other. After a rotation of savage fighting and more than eleven million further transfers, the tempo of the war slackened. Kastellan's offensive had run its course, and the Archflamen had matched it and, at enormous cost, held. In places the Third's regiments could see the Cinder Wall from their trenches, while in others they remained in the ruins of the inner belt. Nor was the Archflamen any longer surrounded. The Third's southern ring had been given up after the Demus Rim, and from the south the Archflamen could send columns around the Third's flank across the open Flats to the east. Far from the trenches, the Third's outrider squadrons fought a hard war of their own on long patrols across the ash to find those columns before they reached anything that mattered.

Another rotation of the siege passed in this way, with heavy losses, little ground and no end in sight. Kastellan had one piece of good news. The Fleet's sweeps down the river and through the low geometry had found no trace of the Eye of Light. The battleship, built at the Forge of [[Hephaestus]] for [[Apollo]]'s fleet under registry AC-09-1642 and driven down through the ceiling at Grid Delta, Sector Nineteen-F, rotations before the revolt, had been kept by the river and had come back up the [[Phlegethon]] with a drowned crew, fought [[UOE Navy|the Fleet]] in the low geometry and ambushed Kastellan's relief convoy. It was now gone from the augurs. Most of the Fleet's officers believed she had withdrawn down the river too badly damaged to risk another engagement, and the cables and convoys to [[Landing Shelf Kappa]] were once more secure.

Against that good news stood the null rate. In Rotation Fourteen the Third's nulls on the Flats passed five percent of its transfers for the first time, and on the leaves within twelve kilometres of the cone they ran at nearly one in five. The Répertoire had by then established that the nulls were concentrated about the [[Echo Mast]], without establishing why, and its technical officers proposed three times that the Mast be made a priority target for the Wrath bombers. Each proposal was refused by the Fleet, whose air officers judged the Mast too strongly defended by the lances on the summit of the cone to be reached, and the Mast went on humming over the Flats.

### 9.4 The Long War (Rotation Thirteen, Cycle 340 to Rotation Fifteen)

After the fighting at the crater, the long war of attrition resumed. Both sides husbanded their strength, reorganised and refitted, and the barrage continued every watch, but for the time being neither could do much more. Kastellan accepted what his offensive had won and drew up plans to recover the ground that had been lost, knowing that the attrition would continue for as long as it had to. The Archflamen's army had joined itself to the deepest things in [[Tartarus (Realm)|Tartarus]]. It would not break or surrender, and it would have to be destroyed to the last soldier.

The Répertoire attempted to count what remained of it. There had been six million, two hundred thousand souls on the spur at the start of the siege, none of them with implants worth the name, so that every one the Third killed was gone for good. Had the Third killed one Arsenal soldier for every five transfers it suffered, the rate assumed in the Répertoire's first plan, then more than seven million had died since the siege began, more than the Arsenal had ever held. The figure was impossible, and the Répertoire's kill counts from the Flats were evidently inflated by a factor nobody could determine. The truth was worse. The Arsenal had been reinforced by the Forsworn, the [[Cinderborn]], the Frost Masons and the Unremembered, by the drowned, and by whatever came up the river at every reformation, and the Archflamen was nowhere near the end of his soldiers. Those soldiers had once been thralls, casting-line gangs and a garrison militia that drilled once a rotation. They were now veterans, hardened by the siege into good soldiers, who believed that something below them was on their side and that victory would be theirs whatever happened at the front. Archflamen Sabinus Orrhae himself had not been seen or heard from in a long time, and nobody in the Third knew whether he still lived in his rooms in the Acropolis. Some of Kastellan's staff proposed sending an Enforcement assassin into the cone after him. Kastellan doubted it would make any difference. He judged it more likely that the Legates of the deep rivers had long since taken the war out of the Archflamen's hands and that the siege was now being fought to their plans.

The Répertoire's revised end-date for the siege had passed nearly two rotations before. The [[Dionysian Logistics Directorate]] had accepted the delay and found the shells for it, but it now undertook another long review of the campaign. The Third had been given twelve rotations and had used them and more without victory. Over Kastellan's objections, the Directorate concluded that it could no longer meet the Third's demands while other fronts wanted the same shells and soldiers. The reduction of the Arsenal would lose its priority. It would not be abandoned, since the Archflamen still had to be defeated and the Arsenal returned to the Empire, but it would take longer and cost more in the end. Kastellan and his staff argued the matter with the Directorate's delegates on Quarterdeck Nineteen to no effect, for the new plan had already been sealed on the ceiling worlds by the Directorate's masters. He would have fewer replacements and fewer shells, and there would be no further review for five rotations. In response, Kastellan sent a delegation of his staff officers up the cables to find help before the Directorate's order began to take effect.

The figures were severe. Under the new plan the Third's replacements from the ceiling worlds were cut from about one hundred and ninety thousand soldiers a rotation to one hundred and ten thousand, and its allocation of shells by two fifths, the balance going to the Cocytus front and the reformation peaks at Depth 12. The Ordnance Phalanxes were placed on a ration of twelve shells per gun per watch outside the preparation of an attack. The Directorate's delegates observed that a siege army which still numbered more than twenty million soldiers could not plead poverty. Kastellan's answer, that an army of the Third's size could not be fed with shells at that rate and would have to stop attacking, was entered in the minutes of the meeting.

### 9.5 The Achillean Order

Kastellan's delegates found it at the relay station of [[Erebus Nine]], a day's flight above the Flats, where the Achillean strike-ship Thetis Mourning was taking on fuel and water after a sweep through the [[Erebus]] shallows in which she had burned out a nest of drakon-breeders in the old flood-tunnels. She carried ninety warriors of the [[Achillean Order]], drawn from four of its lochoi, under [[Teukros Avel|Polemarch Teukros Avel]] of the Third Lochos. The Order was small and old, founded upon a vow made over a shield, and its members were [[Legacy|legacies]] of [[Achilles]], of [[Thetis]] and of the Myrmidons who had followed him. They took the short life. No Achillean carried a Hades implant, and when one died the Order burned the body and said the name, and there was no more. Their founder had been offered a long and obscure life and had chosen the other, and the Order had made the same choice in every generation since. Its warriors were exceptionally hard to kill, and they knew precisely what each death cost.

The whole Order numbered fewer than nine hundred warriors in nine lochoi, and it had never fought in strength on the Tartarus front, whose commanders regarded soldiers who could not be returned as an extravagance the theatre could not afford. The Order fought where it chose, on the ceiling worlds and in the shallows of the deep, and its strike-ships went wherever its Hearth judged that a small number of warriors who did not fear their own deaths could do what larger forces had not. Its relations with the [[Pontifical College]] were poor, since the Order kept its own rites and its own dead and accepted no Flamen, and its relations with the Directorate were nonexistent, since it drew nothing from the Directorate's stores. Kastellan's request reached it through neither, which was in its favour.

Avel heard the delegates with the courtesy the Order showed to all and agreed to examine the situation and put the request to the Order's Hearth on the ceiling, though he declined to commit the strike-ship on his own authority, as the Order's customs would have allowed. Some months later the Hearth's answer reached Quarterdeck Nineteen. Avel's company would come down to the Flats and assess the siege on the ground before deciding where it could do the most good, on the condition that the Polemarch would be under no one's command and would alone decide where and how his warriors fought. Kastellan agreed at once, glad of any help while the Directorate was cutting him off. After their briefing, Avel and his officers held their own council and chose the breach at Leaf 57-44 as the one place on the Flats where ninety warriors could change the outcome of the siege. They named the operation Scaean Gate. Kastellan's staff looked the name up afterward and wished they had not.

### 9.6 Operation Scaean Gate (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 189)

The plan committed everything the Order had, including the strike-ship, to a single fast blow in the dark watch that depended entirely upon its timing. Its point would be the Order's First Lochos, the oldest warriors in the company, in Myrmidon plate forged by the Order's own smiths for each wearer and earned over a lifetime. Avel would lead them himself, with the Order's seer, [[Orrin Kaledos|Mantis Orrin Kaledos]], at his side. They would not cross no-man's land. They would translate through the Thetis Mourning's translation chamber directly from the ship's deck to the crater floor, a procedure that had to be exact, since a translation that missed by twenty metres would place them inside the rubble. The strike-ship would have to come down as close to the Flats as she could, within reach of the [[Ceiling-Lance|ceiling-lances]], and a beacon would have to be placed on the crater floor for the chamber to lock onto. No Achillean could reach the crater floor unseen, so Avel asked Kastellan for a sapper.

The task went to Sapper-Lieutenant Ansel Rook of the Sixty-Sixth Sapper Company, who knew the old galleries the Sixty-First had dug beneath Leaf 57-44 to lay the Great Mine, half-collapsed by the mine itself and half-filled since by the Frost Masons, and believed that one of them still led to a cleft beneath a block on the crater floor. He asked for four volunteers, received eleven offers and took four. A second force under [[Ithra Vell|Lochagos Ithra Vell]] of the Sixth Lochos would stand ready to drive for the crater as soon as the First Lochos was in it, with Kriophoros siege tanks to clear the rubble and three of the Order's Ancients, warriors too badly broken to fight and too stubborn to be burned, who had chosen to go on inside walker-coffins of bronze and iron. They were [[Ancient Aias]], once a priest of the Order, Ancient Rhesos, and Ancient Dolon, who carried a rock-breaking drill and a fire-lance for siege work. Vell's force would hold the crater against the counter-attacks that were certain to follow, and once it was secure Avel would signal the codeword for a battle group of the Ninth Assault Phalanx to pass through the breach and exploit it, accompanied by the Behemoths of Talos under Cradle-Lord Varne in the Anvil of Talos. If all went well, the Order would hand the crater to the Ninth and return to its ship, and for the first time in the siege the Archflamen would face the whole of the Empire's war machine at once. The strike-ship's captain was ordered to survive the ceiling-lances only long enough for the translation and then break off for the high geometry.

Rook's party reached the cleft at the eleventh hour of the dark watch, crawling through galleries half a metre high in places and through the ice with which the Frost Masons had packed the side galleries. At about the same time the Thetis Mourning came down out of the high geometry with her running lights dark. The ceiling-lances found her within four minutes, and she held her course while her wards and her guns fought the beams, two of which burned along her flank. On the crater floor, a sentry discovered Rook's party before the beacon was fully active. The sappers held off the defenders descending the slope while the beacon was brought up by hand, and it locked as the party was reduced to three. Sapper Iolo Brenn was killed and uploaded, and Sapper Kara Nysse was badly wounded.

The First Lochos arrived precisely on the beacon. Thirteen warriors in Myrmidon plate stood up among the dead of seven assaults, and the rounds of the defenders on the slope had no effect upon their armour. They went up the slope with [[Spears|spears]], hammers and great shields, clearing it methodically according to the plan, while in the dark nobody else in the crater could tell what was happening. Kaledos fought at Avel's shoulder, and where he pointed, the defenders' weapons were found already empty or their feet already turned to flight. Those who did not run did not survive. Behind them, Vell's force crossed no-man's land under the Wall's searchlights and guns, its heavy weapons firing into the parapet and the Phaethons' pre-ranged fire falling on the towers, while a Kriophoros destroyed a pillbox the defenders had rebuilt from sandbags and blocks of the Wall. Vell herself was thrown into the air by a shell and went on. Her leading squad reached the rim with Ancient Aias, Dolon's drill broke the largest blocks to open a road for the tanks, and the main body went down through the crater and up its far side under plunging fire without halting.

At the top, the First Lochos met the Laistrygons' charge head on as they came down off the rim in the manner they had used against the Thirty-Fifth. The charge broke upon the Achilleans' shields, and their hammers and spears did to the Laistrygons what no trench soldier had managed. Rook fought beside Avel in that fight with a dead sentry's rifle and a sapper's entrenching blade, and the Order's chroniclers later entered his name in the same column as their own. When Vell and Aias reached their Polemarch, the Order held the crater. In a single dark watch it had done what the Third had failed to do in two rotations, and the garrison had been unable to stop the translation, the speed of the attack or whatever it was that Kaledos did. Avel formed Vell's warriors into a perimeter around the crater and sent the codeword. They had now to hold it until the Ninth and the Behemoths arrived.

### 9.7 Here We Stand (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 190)

The Archflamen had planned for a breach in the Wall and kept a reaction force of tanks and carrier-borne infantry behind it, which his commanders sent in as soon as word of the crater reached them, with the drowned Behemoths to follow. Avel placed his heavy weapons in the gap and held the First Lochos back behind them as a reserve, and under accurate shellfire the Achilleans dug in among the blocks and threw up barricades. The Ninth Assault Phalanx's battle group sent its engineers in first to clear the remaining obstacles with charges and dozers, followed by its tanks, each company with a Colossus, and then its breachers and field guns. At the turn of the watch the Archflamen's counter-attack began. The drowned Behemoths raked the breach from long range, bringing down more of the Wall and destroying one of the Order's transports, and Ancient Aias lost his lance arm to a fragment. The Third's siege guns replied from the rear of the Flats. Myrmidon plate would not stop a Phlegethon-cannon, and the Order's position depended upon Varne's engines arriving before the drowned engines took it apart.

The Anvil of Talos came through the breach first at full stride, followed by two Steropes, its augurs having already found the drowned Behemoths and its gunnery crews already working out their solutions. Its three lance-batteries fired together upon the nearest drowned engine, and the Behemoths fought their own duel while the Order and the Ninth dealt with the Archflamen's armour, and the first of the Ninth's Cyclopean lance-tanks crawled across the crater to join the engagement. The counter-attack pressed hard throughout the cycle. Several times the Archflamen's breachers reached the crater and each time the First Lochos threw them out. Avel was struck by a jet of river-fire from a Phlegethon-lance that burned through his breastplate and his chest behind it, and was carried from the line still giving orders. The Order's healer judged that he would live if the Order's smiths could rebuild what the fire had destroyed. Command of the crater passed to Lochagos Vell and Ancient Aias, and the Order fought on until its ammunition was gone. By then the Ninth had fought the reaction force to a standstill. One of the Steropes was destroyed by a Phlegethon-cannon, its reactor lighting the Flats like a second river, but the drowned engines had been badly damaged in the exchange and withdrew rather than be destroyed. Varne could add another victory over the drowned to the Anvil's tally.

When Vell judged that the Ninth held the breach in a strength nothing the Archflamen possessed could shift, she withdrew the Order across no-man's land on foot and on the hulls of its few remaining transports. Ninety warriors of the Order had gone into [[Operation Scaean Gate]], and thirty-one walked out, many of them badly hurt. Avel was carried. Ancient Aias had lost his arm, and Ancient Dolon had been destroyed by an armour-piercing shell from a Lance-Hunter, his coffin towed back so that the Order's smiths could place another broken warrior in it. The fifty-nine dead were carried out with the living to be burned at the Hearth and their names said before the shield. None of them would return, for the Order had no vats and wanted none. Rook returned with the Order and declined Vell's invitation to join it, on the grounds that he valued coming back too highly. The Thetis Mourning had survived the ceiling-lances, burned along one flank. The Order had paid for the breach with fifty-nine names. Few in the Empire could have done what it did, and the Third knew it. On Leaf 57-44 the Third was through the Cinder Wall at last, and only the Acropolis lay before it.

Kastellan had the Order's dead entered on the roll of the Third as soldiers of the siege, with the note that they had not been transferred and would not be, and sent the roll to the Order's Hearth with his thanks. The Order acknowledged it. It did not reply to his request that the Thetis Mourning remain over the Flats, and she left for the ceiling with her wounded the same cycle.

In the cycles that followed, the Ninth Assault Phalanx widened the lodgement to a frontage of eleven hundred metres and dug it in against the counter-attacks that continued for nine cycles and failed. The lodgement cost the Ninth a further forty thousand transfers. The Répertoire's figures for the whole of the fighting at Leaf 57-44 since the Great Mine came to more than one million, two hundred thousand transfers and sixty-one thousand nulls, besides the fifty-nine names of the Order and the two thousand names of the Deep List. It was the most expensive stretch of ground on the Cinder Flats, and it was the first piece of the Arsenal's last ring the Empire had held in fifteen rotations of siege.

### 9.8 Harbingers and Portents

"Look, she comes up out of the deep with her keys. The old warden of the pit, who kept the [[Hecatoncheires|Hundred-Handed]] for the [[Titans]], and kept them badly, and was paid for it. Fifty heads of beasts go around her waist and they all speak. Her tail has a sting on it the length of a lance, and where it touches the ground the ground forgets it was ever stone.

Behind her comes the mother. She has borne everything that crawls and everything that bites, and she is hungry for her children back. The river carries her like a barge. Her sons walk ahead of her with their heads down and the soldiers who have forgotten their names walk with them.

The pit is opening. The dead in it are counting the living. When the warden turns her keys, the door will be open from below, and nothing on the Flats will close it."

[[Ione Kestrel|Sister-Augur Ione Kestrel]] of the Hunt, a reading on the [[Pyriphlegethon Arsenal]]. Sealed in the Hunt's archive on Luna by order of the [[Artemis|Director]].

### 9.9 The Hunt's Warning (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 197)

On Quarterdeck Nineteen, Kastellan was occupied with the supply calculations for the breach, for the Ninth needed shells, fuel and replacements on the far side of the Wall. His decision to bring in the Achillean Order had succeeded, the Third could see its last objective before it for the first time, and the crisis that had begun on the Demus Rim had been contained. On the hundred and ninety-seventh cycle he received a sealed packet under the crescent seal of the Director of Anti-Espionage and Celestial Surveillance. The [[Hunters of Artemis|Hunt of the Director]] had taken readings on the Tartarean theatre, and every probable future its sister-augurs had searched showed the same thing. The Arsenal was about to break open from below. The Hunt had long known that the Archflamen's soldiers prayed to the deep rivers and that the Legates of those rivers had come up to fight beside him. The readings now showed that the Archflamen's people would soon be offering themselves to the things below as hosts and calling up more of them, and that if they succeeded the Arsenal might not be recoverable at all. The deep geometry beneath Depth 40 was already restless, and the calling-up was nearly complete. The Director held the Tartarean containment lines jointly with the House of Hades and had done so for longer than the Third had existed, and under that charge she was bringing a motion before the Conclave at Watchpoint Hades to take the Third Siege Army under her seal. Kastellan read the packet, put it in his desk and returned to the shell figures.

On the Flats the signs were plain to anyone who could read them, and nobody there could. Wrath crews over the cone reported a light deep within the river cloud beneath the Phlegethon, like a second river burning under the first. The storms of the ceiling geometry flickered without pause. The Echo Mast had begun to hum on frequencies the Third's signallers could not filter. Sentries on the northern front reported soldiers in the Third's grey walking bareheaded through no-man's land in the dark watch toward the Wall, who answered no challenge and were gone when the sentries looked again. Little notice was taken of any of it. The Third would soon face a new enemy on the Flats.

### 9.10 The Director's Choice (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 199)

Two cycles later the Director received Lieutenant [[Thalia Grace]] in the long gallery beneath the [[Sixth Moonwall]] on Luna. No record of the meeting was entered into AtRep. The Director informed Grace that she would present the Hunt's motion to the Conclave in four cycles, with [[Melaina Sarn|Hunt-Elder Sarn]] speaking in support, and that when it passed she would take command of the Third at the Arsenal, with her own contingent under her, and hold it until the Arsenal was closed. Grace objected that she was no politician and that a siege of twenty million soldiers in its fifteenth rotation required a Marshal. The Director replied that the Third already had a Marshal and needed the crescent. Grace then asked whether the posting was a consequence of the [[Themis Needle]], where she had come within a metre of the boy carrying [[Percy Jackson]]'s signature and failed to take him, and the Director confirmed that it was. Grace had frozen on the Needle, having never frozen before, and both of them needed to know whether she would do so again. Tartarus would answer the question. The Hunt has always regarded the appointment as a punishment, and so did Grace. The Director's closing observation, that there were no heights in Tartarus, was understood by everyone in the Hunt who heard of it, since Grace's fear of heights was well known.

### 9.11 The Conclave at Watchpoint Hades (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 203)

On the two hundred and third cycle the Conclave of the Tartarean theatre sat in full session in the old audience hall at Watchpoint Hades, with a long list of business before it, from the removal of a dozen hold quartermasters for theft to the trial of a Haruspex of the Lethe Hold for heresy. First on the list was the Hunt's motion. The siege was in its fifteenth rotation. By the Répertoire's count the Third had transferred more than forty million soldiers on the Cinder Flats, and close to two million had returned blank or not at all, every metre of the Flats bought at a price the Répertoire could state to the gram of slurry. None of that alone would have moved the Hunt. Two things had: the presence of the Legates of the deep rivers on the Flats, and what the Hunt's augurs had read coming up behind them. If the Conclave failed to act, the Third would be lost, then the holds around Depth 40, and then the containment lines themselves, and if the door beneath the Arsenal opened from below, the containment of Tartarus would have a hole in it reaching to the ceiling worlds.

The Conclave was divided. The Pontifical College had sought command of the war since the first rotation. The Archflamen was a Flamen of the College, confirmed by it and preaching in its name, who had spread his heresy among twenty-seven million soldiers under its seal, and the College held that its apostate was its own to judge. The Enforcement delegates sided with the College, as they usually did, since the College would allow Enforcement to run the trials, and between them they held nearly half the votes. The Hunt had sounded out the remainder and found them evenly divided. The Conclave entered to the old oath of the theatre that every soldier of the Third swore at the vats, to hold the line, to keep the door and to come back up, led by the Lord Marshal of the Tartarean Theatre and followed by the delegations of the College, of Enforcement, of the Dionysian Logistics Directorate under Logothete Irene Vasska, of the Forge, of the Pyrrhus Command and of the House of Hades. The Hunt entered last, led by Hunt-Elder Melaina Sarn, who was older than anyone in the hall but the Lord Marshal and more silver alloy than flesh, having lost both legs, an arm and most of her face in the theatre's first Grand Offensive and fought on. Grace followed her with the Director's seal on her pauldron.

Grace's address was short. She told the Conclave that something was coming up beneath the Arsenal, that the Hunt's augurs had read it, and that the delegates had held the readings for two cycles. They could pass the motion and give the Third to the Director so that the Hunt could close the door, or they could send it for another review and argue over whose apostate the Archflamen was, in which case nobody in the hall would be left to judge anybody. The hall erupted, with the College's delegates accusing the Hunt of threatening the Conclave. The debate lasted most of the cycle. Sister-Augur Kestrel was examined for three hours, the reports from the Flats were read in, and the College's delegates spoke against the motion one after another. Twice the Lord Marshal had to call for order, the second time after Hunt-Elder Sarn told the College's senior delegate that she had held the door of Tartarus since before his House was founded and would not hand it to a man who had confirmed the Archflamen in his office.

The vote was as close as everyone had expected. The College voted against the motion to a member, and Enforcement with it, and the outcome came down to the Directorate. Logothete Vasska, who had argued for the removal of the Third's command a dozen rotations earlier and been overruled, abstained, and three of her delegation abstained with her. The motion passed by two votes. The Hunt had its command, but the Conclave left the hall in an angry and suspicious mood. The College had been persuaded of nothing except that the Hunt had taken a war that belonged to it, and it would not forget who had taken its apostate away. Vasska later explained to Grace that she had abstained for the sake of the Third, which somebody had to finish, and that she wanted that somebody to be able to count. Her advice was to begin with the shells.

The consequences of the vote were felt on the ceiling worlds before they reached the Flats. The College's delegation protested the result to Olympia Sacra within the cycle as the seizure of a war of faith by an office of surveillance, and the College let it be known that its temple legions, held back since Rotation Nine against a share in the command, would not now be sent to the Flats on any terms. Enforcement withdrew its Lictor-Superior from Quarterdeck Nineteen, though it left his Lictors in the regimental bunkers. The Directorate made no protest. Vasska's abstention had been her own decision, taken without instruction, and the Directorate's masters let it stand, since it relieved them of a siege they had already decided to starve.

### 9.12 Preparations for a New War (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 203 to 240)

The Director's order went out the same cycle, placing the Third Siege Army under the Hunt of the Director, with Thalia Grace, Lieutenant of the Hunt, in command on the Flats. With her came her own contingent, [[Atlanta]], [[Celyn]], [[Naomi]], [[Josephine]], [[Hemithea]] and [[Phoebe]], with the [[Hounds of Artemis|Hounds]] Columbina and Scaramucius, and thirty-eight further Hunters of the Director's household, each with a small retinue, who would be placed in the command bunker of every regiment on the Flats to observe and, when the time came, to lead from the front. Each could call on the Director's other weapons. There were the [[Psiloi]], the Director's oath-sworn marksmen, who had fought in every war of the Hunt for ten thousand years and could put a round through a Laistrygon's eye at two kilometres, and whose squads would guard the Hunters in the regimental bunkers. There were Enforcement's assassins, whom nobody liked to call upon. And there were the flame hunters and shieldmaidens of the Sixth Moonwall, the oldest fighting companies of the Hunt, trained for nothing except fighting the things that came out of the pit. The plan had been made long before the vote, and a fast moon-ship was already loading the Sixth Moonwall's best companies for the Flats under orders to place themselves at the Lieutenant's disposal.

Grace also sent envoys of her own. Naomi went up to the Achillean Order's Hearth on the ceiling to ask the Order to return in strength and finish what it had begun, as a request, since the Order took no orders. A request also went to the [[Cohors Nemesis Prima|Cohort of Nemesis]] at [[Rhamnous Primaris]], which had fought on the docks of the Phlegethon in the eighth rotation and gone home, though Grace had little hope of it, since the Cohort answered to [[Nemesis]] alone and Nemesis kept her own accounts.

Kastellan was required to give up command of the army at once, with the choice of a post under the Lieutenant or resignation pending a new appointment, without blame or charge, though the Directorate would review his conduct of the siege in the usual way. In the Hunt's judgement he had done well. He had saved the Third from defeat, broken the inner belt and opened the Cinder Wall, and nobody could have asked much more of him. Kastellan asked to be posted to the Flats in command of a regiment, or a cohort if no regiment was available, having fought the siege from Quarterdeck Nineteen for six rotations and wishing to see the Wall from the ground. The Thirty-Fifth had been without a colonel since Osk went null in the crater, and Grace gave it to him the same cycle. A dozen of his staff officers went down with him. Hunt-Elder Sarn also went down to the Flats, having asked for it on the cycle the vote passed. Nobody on the theatre's staff knew more about the things that came out of the pit or how to send them back, and the Arsenal would be her last war against them.

Across the Flats, the colonel of every regiment and attached unit of the Third, down to the Quiet Ones, the cable crews and the reclaimers, received notice that the Hunt of the Director had taken command of the siege and that orders without the crescent seal were to be disregarded. In the trenches little changed. The barrage went over every watch and the Archflamen's soldiers fought for every metre. Far behind the front, however, the Third was being prepared for a different war.

The Hunt's contingent was small beside the army it now commanded. Its Hunters, the Psiloi squads and the companies of the Sixth Moonwall together would not have filled a single Trench Regiment, and they were never intended to fight the siege on their own. Their purpose was to direct the Third against the enemy the Hunt had read beneath the Arsenal, and to stand in front of the Third's soldiers when that enemy came up, since no other force on the Flats had been trained to meet it. The Director's order made the Hunt's authority absolute in matters touching the deep and left the conduct of the trench war to the Third's own officers, an arrangement that the Third's colonels, after six rotations of Kastellan and eight of [[Aurelian Castorre|Castorre]], accepted with less complaint than the Conclave had expected.

### 9.13 Landing Shelf Kappa (Rotation Fifteen, Cycle 241)

On the two hundred and forty-first cycle, Grace came down the cable to Landing Shelf Kappa in a supply car with her contingent, the Hounds and forty tonnes of shells, a descent of nine minutes she made with her eyes shut. From Kappa the cone of the Acropolis stood twenty kilometres away, with the Echo Mast on its summit and the red river cloud behind it, and between the shelf and the cone the trenches of fifteen rotations crossed the ash from one horizon to the other. A signals officer met the Hunt on the shelf with the compliments of Colonel Kastellan of the Thirty-Fifth and a request from Commander Thaleia of Lethe Hold, whose signals and the Lieutenant's had already begun to go astray, that the Hunt's traffic carry the Lieutenant's full name and seal to distinguish them. Grace agreed, and signed for command of the Third on the shelf the same watch.

## 10 Closing the Ring

> *"To fight the thing that eats the soul and come away whole, you keep your purpose narrow. Each of us conquers herself first. Then the rest."*
>
> Hunt-Elder Melaina Sarn, to the Hunters of the Third, before the cleansing of the Arsenal

### 10.1 The Moonwall Comes Down (Rotation Sixteen, Cycle 101)

On the hundred and first cycle of Rotation Sixteen, the moon-ship [[Selene's Arrow]] came down through the high geometry over the Flats, eleven cycles out from Luna, carrying the first company of the Sixth Moonwall under Shield-Captain Antheia [[Shield-Captain Roe|Roe]]. She was among the fastest vessels in the Empire and heavily armed for her size, and her translation chambers could put several squads on the ground at once. Only the Hunt flew ships of her kind. Her pilots came from the Hunt's own houses of navigators, bound to the Director by oath since before the Empire had a name, who could take a ship through the deep geometry by feel on routes no pilot of the Fleet would attempt. With them the Hunt could place a strike force anywhere in the Empire faster than any other institution, and the arrival of the Moonwall gave Lieutenant Grace a reserve that answered to her alone.

### 10.2 The Third under the Crescent

The Third that Grace inherited at the start of Rotation Sixteen was a different army from the one Castorre had led down the Cinder Stair. It had fought for sixteen rotations of the theatre's clock and consumed more than sixty million transfers, and of the soldiers who had come down the Stair in Rotation Zero, the Répertoire estimated that fewer than one in five were still fit for the forward bays. The rest had gone null, or had been transferred so often that the vats returned them slow, or had been posted to the support line, the wire teams and the depots at Kappa. The regiments were kept at strength by drafts from the ceiling worlds, younger each rotation, and by the soldiers coming back down the cables from Watchpoint Hades, many of them on their twentieth or thirtieth return. On paper the Third numbered forty-one Trench Regiments, nineteen ordnance regiments and eleven armoured regiments. In practice a third of the Trench Regiments were below half strength, and the Tenth Ordnance Phalanx was firing ceiling-made shells of uneven quality, since the Arsenal's own stocks had been exhausted in Rotation Eleven.

The arrangement of command was also new. The Director's order had placed the Third under the Hunt, and the Hunt had no tradition of commanding trench armies. Grace kept Kastellan's staff on Quarterdeck Nineteen intact and left the phalanx commands in the hands of the officers who already held them, setting her own contingent of Hunters over them as a second chain that could overrule the first. Kastellan, who had been reduced to the colonelcy of the Thirty-Fifth by the Conclave's settlement, retained the confidence of the Third's officers, and Grace was careful to consult him before every major operation. The relationship between them remained correct and cool. Kastellan regarded the Hunt as an instrument of the Director's displeasure, and Grace regarded herself in much the same light, since she had been sent to the Flats as a punishment and knew that the whole of the Hunt knew it. Neither allowed the matter to affect the conduct of the siege. The Hunt's staff were nonetheless struck by how quickly the Lieutenant learned the trade of the trench war, and by her readiness to spend her own Hunters at the front where a Lord-General would have kept them back.

The Hunt brought with it capabilities the Third had never possessed. Its sister-augurs could read the deep in a way the Répertoire could not, and for the first time the Third had warning of reformations and of what the Legates of the rivers were preparing. The Hunters themselves could fight the things that came out of the river on equal terms, and the Psiloi, the Hunt's light troops, were the finest marksmen in the Empire. What the Hunt lacked was numbers. Grace had fewer than two hundred Hunters on the Flats at the start of the rotation and the two companies of the Moonwall still to come, against an enemy that the Répertoire still counted in millions. The siege would have to be won by the Third, and the Hunt's task was to make sure that it was.

The first problem facing Grace and her new staff was the one that had occupied Kastellan for most of a rotation. The siege could not be finished while the east remained open. The ring around the Acropolis had a gap in it, and while the gap remained the Archflamen had a line of retreat. Even if the Third took the cone, the war might continue elsewhere if his people could leave it and fight on from the deep holds. The open flank also tied down the [[Fifth Line Phalanx]], which had to guard the rear of the First Line Phalanx against attack from the east, so that good regiments and good guns were held on the defensive when they were needed elsewhere. Grace's solution was to close the ring.

The four regiments of the Fifth Line Phalanx would attack in echelon from north to south, first the Twenty-Second, then the Twenty-Fourth and the Twenty-Sixth, and finally the Twenty-Eighth. They would swing south and then south-west to close the ring by joining the Thirty-Fifth Trench Regiment at Leaf 57-44, the southernmost regiment on the [[Seventh Line Phalanx]]'s front, which Kastellan now commanded. The whole movement would turn like a door upon a hinge, with the First Line Phalanx's positions as the hinge. From the north, the attack had more than sixty kilometres to cover, and the outrider patrols reported the whole of it held by the Unremembered and the Archflamen's Lethe-blooded cohorts. To give the Fifth an armoured fist for the left hook, the Sixteenth and [[Twentieth Armoured Regiment|Twentieth Armoured]] Regiments were attached to it. The other regiments of the Third were to hold their lines, patrolling and raiding, until the Acropolis was enclosed in a ring of guns. The Hunt expected the Unremembered to come out of the mist to meet the attack, and Hunters would accompany the offensive, while Shield-Captain Roe's company waited on the Selene's Arrow as a fast reserve to be sent wherever the Archflamen was strongest or the things from the pit appeared. The attack was set for the hundred and fiftieth cycle.

Two further operations would follow if the hook succeeded. In the first, the three remaining regiments of the First Line Phalanx would advance to the Cinder Wall on the north side and pin its defenders against it. In the second, the Eleventh Line Phalanx would come down from the north to take the high ground there, a ridge of old cinder vents that the Third's maps called Height 202. The Eleventh was the least experienced of the Third's phalanxes, and its three regiments had been on the Flats for only six rotations. When both operations were complete, the ring would have closed up to the Wall along its whole length, and the Archflamen would be confined in a pocket that could only grow smaller.

### 10.3 The Grey (Rotation Sixteen, Cycle 150)

The Fifth Line Phalanx would not be attacking a fixed line. In front of it lay ground that had been fought over for rotations and was covered in the wreckage of that fighting, with old tanks and guns half buried in the ash or tipped into shell holes beside the broken remains of trenches and pillboxes, and stretches of wire that had once guarded something and now guarded nothing. Beneath the ash lay unmapped [[Mines (explosive)|mines]] and unexploded shells, either of which could detonate without warning after rotations in the ground. On the hundred and fiftieth cycle, with a seep-storm coming in over the [[Lethe]] Cut, the Fifth's batteries put down a rolling barrage mixed with [[Smoke (SMK)|smoke]], and the Twenty-Second's leading cohorts advanced behind it, each with a company of the [[Sixteenth Armoured Regiment|Sixteenth Armoured]] joined to it as a battle group.

At first the advance went well. Then the Archflamen's counter-barrage came down, and among the smoke of its bursts was a paler and heavier cloud that did not rise. It was Grey Seventh. The Third had known the distillate since the last stand of the [[Fifty-Fourth Trench Regiment|Fifty-Fourth]] on the Lethe Cut, where a hundred and nine thousand of that regiment's soldiers had gone null and thirty-two thousand more had gone absent, but it still had no understanding of what the substance did beyond the fact that soldiers who breathed it began to forget, and soldiers who died in it reached Watchpoint Hades blank or did not reach it at all. The cloud lay across the Twenty-Second's front, and its attack fell into confusion. Cohorts lost their way in the smoke, and sections that walked into the thickest of the Grey simply stopped and stood in the ash, unable to say where they were or what they had come to do. The relay at the Twenty-Second's command bunker logged one in three of the uploads from the dead in the cloud as empty.

The tanks, sealed against the Grey, became the point of the attack. Visibility inside the cloud was a few metres. Tanks drove into unseen shell holes and old tank ditches and stuck, and several Thureos struck mines. The Archflamen's cohorts counter-attacked through the cloud, and the fighting that followed was a series of small and confused actions in which Arsenal tanks fired blind or closed to point-blank range, burning whole squadrons before pulling back. The Third could not coordinate tanks, infantry and guns inside the Grey, and the Archflamen's crews won most of those actions. They lacked the strength to stop the attack. Its weight kept it moving, and wherever the Grey thinned the Twenty-Second pressed on against scattered resistance, gaining ten kilometres by the end of the first cycle. On the second cycle the Twenty-Fourth joined the attack to its south.

For the first few cycles the offensive continued, with the Archflamen feeding soldiers into its path wherever he could and the Fifth's armoured battle groups driving on. On the fourth cycle resistance stiffened, as the Archflamen's commanders organised a proper defence. The Unremembered came up out of the Lethe Cut in force with their own armour, and behind them came things the Third had not yet met on the Flats: Laistrygons who had been in the Grey too long and no longer knew what they were, blind drakon-spawn from the deep river channels, and other creatures without names that came out of the mist for the tanks.

When the Twenty-Sixth joined the battle, it reported that it was fighting the Third's own soldiers. They came out of the Grey in the dark watch in the Third's grey coats, without helmets or rifles, walking toward the Twenty-Sixth's trenches in a long loose crowd that did not answer a challenge or go to ground when fired upon. Some had died in the Grey and stood up again in the ash after their uploads had reached Hades blank. Others had never died at all. They were the absent of the Fifty-Fourth, the thirty-two thousand the Répertoire had been unable to account for, and they still wore their regiment's flash. They now fought for the Archflamen. The Twenty-Sixth called them the Hollowed. They were killed in hundreds and kept coming regardless of their losses, and some of those cut down rose again and had to be destroyed repeatedly. Those who reached the parapet fought with hands and teeth, and the Twenty-Sixth found that its soldiers hesitated before shooting a figure wearing the Third's colours. The Hollowed did not hesitate.

Hunt-Elder Sarn examined the Hollowed on the Twenty-Sixth's front on the sixth cycle and reported to Grace that they were Lethe-work. The Grey removed the soldier and left the body, and the Unremembered put something else into it, whose nature she could not yet determine. The Hollowed could not be recovered, and she did not yet know whether the process could be stopped. Grace's note on the report identified the strategic danger. If the Third's dead fought for the Archflamen, he would have as many soldiers as the Third gave him, and no army could fight a war of attrition against an enemy that grew with every loss it inflicted. Soon the Twenty-Fourth and the Twenty-Second were reporting Hollowed on their fronts as well.

After eight cycles of fighting, the Fifth Line Phalanx's offensive had stuck in the Grey. The ground taken in the first cycles was held, but the Twenty-Second's long hook had reached only halfway to its objective, the enemy in front of the Fifth was now nearly equal to it in numbers, and more Hollowed emerged from the Grey every watch. To break the deadlock, the Hunters with the Fifth authorised the use of the Third's own proscribed stocks. The Third had kept a reserve of Styx-salt shells since the Second Grand Offensive, sealed in a deep magazine at Kappa and listed as destroyed. Styx-salt burned the Lethe out of anything it touched, and the Hollowed it touched lay down and did not rise. It also poisoned the ground and the air above it for centuries. Leaves 59-45 and 60-45 were drowned in it, and the Répertoire estimated that they would not be fit to stand upon for four hundred years. The hook closed. The cohorts of the Ninth Assault Phalanx that had supported the drive were withdrawn to refit and sent to support the First Line Phalanx's offensive, while the Tenth Ordnance Phalanx's assault batteries moved into position for the Eleventh Line Phalanx's attack on Height 202. Both attacks would begin on the two hundred and thirtieth cycle, and the Hunters would lead both.

The hook had closed the ring, but the cost of closing it was measured in more than transfers. The Fifth Line Phalanx had lost two hundred and forty thousand soldiers in eight cycles, and the Répertoire logged sixty-one thousand of them as null and nine thousand as absent, the first time the Third had used the second category in a report since the Lethe Cut. The decision to use Styx-salt was also a political one. The Pontifical College protested to the [[Olympian High Council|Council of the Twelve]] that the Hunt had poisoned ground within sight of the [[Katabasion]], and the Dionysian Logistics Directorate complained that two leaves of the Flats had been removed from any future use. Grace answered both in a single paragraph, which stated that the leaves in question had been in use by the enemy, and that the Third's dead were no longer available to fight for him. The protests were noted. They were also remembered, and the College would return to them before the rotation was out.
