The girl, identified from the Thirty-Fifth's rolls as the relay engineer Verrill Chase, attached herself to the signal companies and the sappers and was soon in demand along the whole of the southern front.

### 12.3 The Return of the Achilleans (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 159)

On the hundred and fifty-ninth cycle of Rotation Eighteen, the Hunt's earlier embassy to the Achillean Order was repaid. Hunter Naomi had gone up to the Order's Hearth on the ceiling as instructed and placed the Lieutenant's request directly into the hands of the Order's Archon, [[Damar Orthes]], who took it to his council. No order of the Empire committed itself to a fight lightly, and the Achilleans least of all. They carried no implants, every warrior they lost was lost for good, and they were few enough that each had to be spent with care. Orthes nonetheless knew the Arsenal already. Polemarch Avel's company had returned from Operation Scaean Gate half destroyed and victorious, and the Hunt was now asking the Order to finish what Avel had begun. The Hunt could have ordered it, since the Director's seal reached that far, and Grace had chosen instead to ask, which the Order noted. The Achilleans were bound to the Empire by their own oath and nothing else, and they regarded oath-breakers as the worst of enemies. The Forsworn were on the Flats. It was this argument, put by Naomi, that carried the council.

Orthes assembled the whole Order before the shield in the Hearth and announced that he would lead a force back to the Arsenal himself, large enough to finish the work Avel had started. Four lochoi would go, four hundred warriors in all, and the Order's great ship, the [[Patroklos Unburied]], was made ready to carry them. The Order prepared for war as it had done thousands of times before. Its priests warned every warrior that the Arsenal had become a haunt of the deep, where they would face the river as well as the Archflamen's guns, and that the river would offer them things. The Order's teaching held that a fight against the deep had to be won inside each warrior as well as on the field. The Ancients were woken in their coffins, Ancient Aias among them with a new arm, together with Ancient Rhesos and the broken warrior who now walked in Dolon's rebuilt shell, and the Order's smiths blessed every engine in the armoury. Polemarch Avel left the smiths' hall on his own feet, with a new chest of bronze and iron under his plate, and took his place at the head of the First Lochos.

Damar Orthes had been Archon of the Order for sixty-one years, an unusually long tenure for a warrior without implants, and his reputation among the Olympian orders was that of a commander who chose his battles with care and then fought them without restraint. He had led the Order through the Erebus shallows campaign and the clearance of the old flood-tunnels under [[Hold Serapis]], and he had a low opinion of the Répertoire, which he regarded as an institution that spent soldiers because it could replace them. The Order had no such luxury. Its warriors were raised from children of Achilles and Thetis's line who had refused the implant, and the Order's law held that a warrior who died in battle had paid in full and could not be asked to pay again. Orthes's decision to commit four lochoi to a siege the Third had been losing for eighteen rotations was therefore a considerable one, and the Order's council recorded it as the largest commitment of its strength to a single field in three centuries.

The decision was also political. The Achilleans held their Hearth on the ceiling by an old charter of the Council of the Twelve, and the Dionysian Logistics Directorate had twice in the preceding century proposed that the Order be brought under the Répertoire's accounting like every other formation of the Tartarus front. The Hunt of the Director had opposed both proposals. Orthes was aware that an Order which answered the Hunt's call at the Arsenal would have a friend at the [[Conclave at Watchpoint Hades|Conclave]] when the Directorate next raised the matter, and the Hunt's later correspondence makes clear that Grace was aware of it as well.

The Patroklos Unburied arrived over the Flats on the hundred and fifty-ninth cycle and landed the whole force on the Slag Gate spur by cable and lander. Orthes met Grace in her bunker and placed his force at the Hunt's disposal, and after a briefing the two commanders went into council alone. Orthes studied the war-table model of the cone at length. The Acropolis was a terrible objective, but the Order's pride required it to take the hardest task the Lieutenant could give it, and Orthes asked for the honour of the first breach into the cone itself.

It was agreed that the Achilleans would attack up the Pilgrim Stair from the ruins of the Slag Gate spur to take and hold the Pilgrim Gate. The attack would go straight up the stair, under the guns of the cone's southern walls for every step of the climb. The Pilgrim Gate was a bastion of cut stone with its own guard towers. Its outer gate opened onto a long tunnel, certain to be a killing ground, which led to an inner gate and then to a colonnade running to the Katabasion. The wards had so far kept the Phaethons from doing more than scar the face of the gatehouse, and its interior defences were intact. The first objective would be the bunkers cut into the cliff where the stair turned at its first landing, which were given to the leap-squads of three lochoi under Lochagos Ithra Vell. Once the bunkers were engaged, Orthes would lead the main column up the stair in the Order's heavy assault carriers at speed, covered by the Order's rocket batteries and strike craft firing on the walls to suppress the defenders. The warriors not committed to the stair would crew the Order's siege tanks and battle tanks in a diversionary attack on the Founders' Gate.

The plan had one serious difficulty, which Orthes identified himself. The Polemarch's First Lochos was the Order's translation lochos, and it could not translate into a warded gatehouse without a beacon inside the wards. The Order had no means of placing one there. While the council was still sitting, a signal carrying no seal appeared on the war table on the Hunt's own channel.

> BEACON WILL BE IN THE OUTER TUNNEL OF THE PILGRIM GATE AT YOUR ZERO HOUR. TELL THE POLEMARCH.

Orthes asked who had sent it. Grace could not tell him. The plan went ahead with the beacon included.

### 12.4 The Plan for the Gates

The Achilleans would not attack alone. Grace's plan committed every force the Third could still bring against the cone to a simultaneous assault on all three gates on the hundred and seventy-first cycle. On the western face, the Fiftieth Trench Regiment would renew its attack on the Founders' Gate under a rolling barrage, supported by the Anvil of Talos, the last battle group of Forge Cohort Talos under Cradle-Lord Doros Varne, which still had four Behemoths in service. The Order's armour would join the Fiftieth on the lower switchbacks. On the north face, the Hunt of the Director would attack the Flamen Gate with the Sixth Moonwall and the shock breachers of the Forty-Third and Fiftieth Trench Regiments, covered by the Steropes-pattern Bellows of the Forge on the lower slope. On the southern face, the Achilleans would take the Pilgrim Gate. The Thirty-Fifth Trench Regiment under Colonel Kastellan would hold the Gully of Ash and the vault doors, so that nothing could come out of the magazines into the flank of the Founders' Gate attack, and the remaining regiments of the Third would hold the ring around the foot of the cone.

The plan's logic was that of every general assault of the siege, refined by eighteen rotations of failure. No single gate could be taken while the defenders were free to reinforce it from the outer ward, and the outer ward was small enough that its defenders could reach any gate within the hour. Three attacks at once would give them no such freedom. The Founders' Gate attack was intended to draw the Archflamen's walkers and furnace-engines onto the western switchbacks, where the Behemoths could engage them on open ground, and the Hunt's staff were prepared for it to fail so long as it held the defenders there. The Flamen Gate was the decisive objective, since it gave directly onto the outer ward, and the Pilgrim Gate attack would place a force at the far end of the colonnade from which the Katabasion could be reached.

The preparations took twelve cycles. The Directorate's reductions had left the Third short of shells, and Grace's staff drew on the reserves stockpiled for the assault on the Scree and on the Fleet, which for the first time could bring its guns to bear on the cone from the low geometry. One million, four hundred thousand shells were brought forward to the gun lines around the foot of the cone, less than half the stockpile for the first assault on the outer belt in Rotation One. The Phaethons were to fire on the wards over the gates for three cycles before the assault, to wear them down as far as they could be worn, and the Fleet would add its fire on the morning of the attack. The Hunt's sister-augurs were assigned to every assault force, with orders to read the deep on the cone during the attack and to warn the commanders of anything coming up the gorge. Their readings on the eve of the assault were unanimous that something larger than anything yet seen at the Arsenal was close to the surface, and Sister-Augur Kestrel advised Grace that her reading of Rotation Fifteen was about to be fulfilled. Grace kept the assault on schedule. The Third could not wait for the deep to show itself, and the Hunt's staff judged that the Flamen Gate force, with the Moonwall and the Hunters of the household, was the force best able to meet whatever came.

### 12.5 Battle for the Pilgrim Gate (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 171)

The assault on the Pilgrim Gate began on the hundred and seventy-first cycle with the attack on the bunkers covering the stair. The bunkers were cast basalt, built to resist plunging shells, and every leap-squad carried breaching charges to open them. A strike craft of the Patroklos Unburied came in low and dropped fire-bombs onto the position, and when the fire burned out Lochagos Vell's squads went off a rubble heap that had once been part of the Slag Gate's wall in a single long bound and came down out of the smoke onto the bunkers with their leap-rigs blazing. As they went in, Orthes ordered the armoured column forward to its start line. A second strike craft fired a salvo of missiles into the gatehouse, and the Order's rocket batteries fired along the walls of the cone, where the wards flared under the impacts.

With the bunkers falling, the main column moved off with three heavy assault carriers leading, Orthes riding in the first. The road to the stair had been broken by rotations of shelling, and the carriers churned through the paste to its foot and then up it, with every gun firing at the walls above. The Pilgrim Stair was a single route that pilgrims had climbed in file for eight thousand years, with the cliff rising to the ramparts on one side and a sheer drop to the Flats on the other, and at each flight of steps the drivers forced the carriers into the old stone until the tracks bit. Fire from the walls fell on the hulls throughout the climb. Less than halfway up, the leading carrier was hit and slewed across the stair burning, its assault ramp jammed. Orthes forced the ramp down by hand with two of his warriors and led them out on foot. The second carrier pushed the wreck aside and struck a hidden mine that took off its right track, and the column could go no further. The rest of the Order dismounted and followed the Archon's standard up the stair through the fire.

The Archflamen's soldiers came down out of the Pilgrim Gate to meet them, led once again by the Brethren of the Black Shale in their dark plate, and the fighting on the ramps and steps was savage. Orthes is recorded throwing one of the Brethren from the stair and cutting a second in half. The Order's strike craft dived on the defenders crowding the steps, and the Ancients, climbing where no carrier could, came up behind the warriors and drove the Archflamen's soldiers back up the stair with their heavy guns. Within minutes hundreds of bodies lay along the steps. The Order halted under the gate-towers to regroup while the defenders withdrew inside and barred the outer gate. The Achilleans had reached their objective. They now had to storm it.

At zero hour the beacon was in the outer tunnel, as the signal had promised. How it came to be there was never established by the Order. Psiloi of the Hunt watching the cliff below the gatehouse reported a figure climbing the bare rock under the walls during the fighting on the stair, which they at first took for a lizard of the deep. It climbed two hundred metres of cliff in four minutes and entered through a gun slit the width of a hand, cracked by the Phaethons and never repaired. Survivors of the gate's garrison, taken later, reported that a boy in a burned coat came out of the gun gallery into the outer tunnel carrying an Achillean translation beacon, set it on the floor and opened it, and that every round they fired at him stopped in the air a hand's breadth from his body. The Order's net log records a single transmission informing the Polemarch that he had a lock. From the translation chamber of the Patroklos Unburied, Polemarch Avel and the First Lochos translated into the gatehouse.

The Archflamen's soldiers had been driven from the stair but were not beaten, and behind them in the bastion waited what the Kindlers had raised. The outer gate opened again and the deep came out of the tunnel mouth: the Burned, soldiers of the reformation who walked as fire in the shape of men, hounds of the deep the size of carriers with river-fire in their mouths, and khalkotauroi, the bronze bulls of the old forges, together with drakon-spawn, mad thralls and the Kindled, the Lictors who had gone down in the Gully of Ash and come back changed beyond recognition. The fighting surged back and forth across the steps under the bastion, and the Achilleans were hard pressed. The First Lochos could not come to their aid, since it was already fighting its way out of the outer tunnel into the backs of the creatures coming down the steps. The Order's plate gave little protection against what it faced, and many warriors fell. The standard-bearer was trampled by a bronze bull until a lance-pistol shot burst its furnace. Ancient Aias went down on the steps with three hounds of the deep on his coffin. The priest of the Second Lochos chanted the Order's oath throughout with her staff raised, and the Order held.

Orthes was wounded and dragged clear by the Order's healer, his helm and blade lost, and for a time it appeared that the Achilleans would have to fall back or be destroyed. The situation was restored by Lochagos Vell, who, with her work at the bunkers done, came off the first landing with what remained of her squad and struck the Burned from behind. Her charge broke them, and the thralls behind them fled. Her squad's last breaching charges blew a hole in the outer gate, and the Achilleans went through it. In the outer tunnel Vell found the First Lochos fighting with their backs to the beacon and the boy among them, with one hand on the tunnel wall. Her helm recorded that he told her the inner gate ran on a counterweight and that he would have it open in thirty seconds, and that he declined to answer when she asked who he was. Twenty-eight seconds later the inner gate rose and the Order went through.

Orthes, having refused his healer and taken up a fallen blade, rejoined the fighting inside. The Achilleans cleared the bastion floor by floor and showed no mercy to anything left in it, and by the end of the cycle the gatehouse was theirs. They sealed its entrances and swore to hold it against whatever came. Orthes climbed to the roof of the bastion and planted the Order's torn standard there in view of every soldier on the Flats, and as he did so the first salvoes of the Patroklos Unburied began to fall on the wards of the Acropolis from the low geometry, now open to the Fleet with most of the ceiling-lances destroyed. When Vell looked for the boy afterward, he was gone.

The gate cost the Order most of the force it had committed. Of the four hundred warriors who had come down from the Patroklos Unburied, two hundred and thirty-one lay dead along the Pilgrim Stair and against the gatehouse walls, and of the remainder only a hundred and twelve were judged fit to fight. Ancient Aias was recovered from the steps and returned to his coffin, the third time in his long service that he had been carried off a field. The Achilleans had no vats to return to, and every one of the dead was gone for good. The Order did not ask to be relieved, and Grace did not offer it. For the remaining cycles of the siege the Achilleans held the Pilgrim Gate with what they had, against counter-attacks from the colonnade on six occasions, and they were still holding it when the final assault began.

### 12.6 Battle for the Founders' Gate (Rotation Eighteen, Cycles 171 to 174)

On the western face of the cone, the attack on the Founders' Gate went much as the Hunt's staff had expected. The Fiftieth Trench Regiment went up the lower switchbacks behind its rolling barrage at the first bell, with the Order's siege tanks and battle tanks on the road and the Anvil of Talos following below. The Founders' Gate stood at the top of eleven switchbacks, each commanded by a tower of the outer ward, and the road had been mined and cratered so often in the course of the siege that only its lower half could still be used by armour. The Fiftieth took the first four switchbacks in the opening watch, at the cost of nine thousand transfers, and then met the counter-attack the plan had been designed to provoke.

The Archflamen's walkers and furnace-engines came down the road from the Founders' Gate in strength, the largest concentration of the enemy's remaining armour seen since the Scree, and with them came the iron machines into which the Cinderborn had sealed living people. The Behemoths of the Anvil of Talos engaged them on the open ground of the lower switchbacks, as Grace had intended, and over the following two cycles the Anvil destroyed thirty-eight walkers and furnace-engines on the road. The Order's diversionary force held the fourth switchback against them throughout. The Fiftieth could make no further progress up the road, and on the third cycle the attack was halted below the sixth switchback, with the gate itself untouched.

The Founders' Gate attack had cost the Fiftieth thirty-one thousand transfers, the Anvil of Talos one Behemoth and the Order eleven of its tanks, and it had failed to take its objective. It had nonetheless done what was required of it. For the duration of the assault on the cone the enemy's walkers and furnace-engines were held on the western face, and none of them reached the Pilgrim Stair or the north slope, where the decisive fighting took place.

### 12.7 Battle for the Flamen Gate (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 171)

While the Achilleans fought up the Pilgrim Stair, the Hunt's own assault force gathered for its attack on the Flamen Gate on the north face of the cone. To the west, the rolling barrage ahead of the Fiftieth Trench Regiment's renewed attack on the Founders' Gate was already pounding the slope and wrapping the Acropolis in grey dust. Lieutenant Grace's force was the strongest the Hunt could assemble. It comprised eight Hunters of the Director's household with their full retinues, the Psiloi squads, and companies of shock breachers from the Forty-Third and Fiftieth Trench Regiments. The Sixth Moonwall's two companies went up in the Hunt's silver carriers under Shield-Captain Antheia Roe and Flame-Captain Dione Tharn, and Hunt-Elder Sarn walked with them on her metal legs. Wrath wings stood by to cover the final approach.

The assault's communications were the work of Verrill Chase, who accompanied it as a relay engineer. She had come over from the Thirty-Fifth the cycle before and warned the Forty-Third's signal officer that his relay chain would fail as soon as the assault entered the gate tunnels, which ran sixty metres into the rock. Below eighty metres no upload reached a relay, and between sixty and eighty they were unreliable. Chase proposed a chain of relay sets laid up the slope and into the tunnels behind the assault at forty-metre intervals, so that the three-second cadence could find its way out. Asked who she was, she identified herself as the engineer who had fixed the masks. She was given two signallers and spent the assault in a trench at the foot of the slope with a stack of relay sets. A signaller in that trench later reported that she recognised Grace on sight as the Thalia Grace of the old stories and began describing her aloud with some enthusiasm, until every relay set in the trench spoke at once in a single voice and ordered her to be quiet, which she was.

Before the advance, Grace walked among the breachers while her priests gave every soldier the old vow against what they were about to face. On her word the whole force rose and advanced up the slope beside its carriers at walking pace. As it climbed, the ceiling over the Flats went black and a storm broke beneath it. There is no rain on the Cinder Flats. The storm was lightning alone, crossing the geometry under the ceiling from one horizon of the Flats to the other in wave after wave as the Hunt went up toward the gate, and every soldier on the slope reported a weight in the air.

The defenders came out of the Flamen Gate to meet the assault in a mass of red and bronze: the Burned and the Kindled with the bronze bulls and the hounds, and furnace-engines walking among them spitting molten iron from their cannon. They chanted as they came and kept coming through the breachers' fire. The two forces met on the slope. The Moonwall came out of their carriers straight into the fighting, their spears burning cold, and the Burned went out in plumes of steam where the spears went in, while lightning came down out of the ceiling wherever Grace pointed her spear. The hounds dragged down many of the Hunt's soldiers, but the Moonwall cut its way up after the Lieutenant, and a furnace-engine fell with a Moonwall spear in its workings. When the last of the Burned went out, the Flamen Gate lay open before the Hunt.

### 12.8 The Warden of Tartarus

At that moment Kampe came up out of the gorge that splits the cone. She rose from the Phlegethon far below on black wings, in a column of river-fire that climbed past the Echo Mast and lit the whole of the Flats red. Kampe was the warden of Tartarus, the jailer who had kept the Hundred-Handed in the pit for the Titans before the Olympians were born. The Kindlers' door had let her up, as Sister-Augur Ione Kestrel's reading had predicted in Rotation Fifteen. She settled on the ramparts of the Flamen Gate and crouched there to look at the battle. From the waist up she was a woman as tall as a Behemoth's command deck, with a head of black snakes, and around her waist grew the living heads of fifty beasts. Below the waist she was a dragon, with a scorpion's tail rising behind her and a sting as long as a lance, and she carried a curved blade in each hand smoking with green poison. Shells burst around her without visible effect.

Kampe came down off the parapet onto the slope, and her first target was the Steropes-pattern Bellows of the Forge that had walked up the lower slope behind the Hunt. Her first blow cut through its wards and plate, her second broke it open, and its reactor exploded. She went on into the Hunt's force, sweeping aside everything in reach. A Hunt carrier that could not reverse out of her path was cut open and burned, and two Psiloi were killed by a single stroke of her tail. Flame-Captain Tharn charged her with her company. The Moonwall's cold fire was the first thing in the battle to hurt the warden, and Tharn's company poured it into her until a storm of cold light surrounded the fight. Kampe struck back blindly and cut down the shieldmaidens one by one. Tharn held her for some time while the cold fire drained her own strength, and at the end gathered what remained of it into her spear and met a blow from Kampe's blade with it. The spear broke in a burst of light that lit the whole slope, and Tharn fell disarmed and badly hurt. Her last two shieldmaidens stood in front of her. Kampe threw one down the slope and carried the other up into the storm. The Flame-Captain's company had been broken.

While Kampe fought the Moonwall, Grace had gathered every survivor she could and led them toward the gate. Most of the gate and its two towers had been reduced to rubble by the Phaethons, and the fire-shells had cleared the walls on either side of defenders. Kampe came down out of the storm into the ruined gateway with her wings spread, dropped the captive shieldmaiden on the stones and screamed her challenge at the slope. The breachers broke and fell back. Grace went up the last of the slope into the gateway alone.

### 12.9 The Lieutenant and the Warden

The Hunt's chronicle describes the duel that followed as a contest between speed and lightning on one side and strength on the other. The warden's blows split the stone of the gateway, and Grace turned them repeatedly while the bolts she called came down wherever her spear touched and burned black marks across the scales. Both were wounded. Kampe had fought for longer than there had been [[God (greek)|gods]], and she did not tire. One of her blades caught Grace across the left arm, and the poison numbed it to the shoulder. Grace threw herself flat under a stroke of the tail and came onto her back with the warden standing over her, both blades raised.

The blow was stopped by a shield that no one on the slope could see. Witnesses describe a shimmer in the air a hand's breadth thick, like heat over the Flats, on which both of Kampe's blades stopped dead, and a shock that went out through the gateway in a ring and threw the dust off every stone. The boy was standing over Grace with one hand raised. It was the first direct use in the siege of the Aegis, the shield the Hunt's files had recorded on the Themis Needle, by the intelligence those files designated Minerva. The helm recordings from the gateway show that Kampe recognised him, or believed she did, and named him as the boy who had stolen her prisoner. The prisoner was [[Briares]] the Hundred-Handed, freed from Kampe's keeping by Percy Jackson in the [[Titanomachy|Titan War]] fourteen thousand years before. Minerva answered only that Kampe had known someone, ordered the Lieutenant to her feet and directed her to the warden's left side. Grace recognised the face she had chased up the Themis Needle, and complied.

They attacked together. Kampe struck at each in turn, and the Aegis turned each blow. Grace went in under the warden's left arm where the shield had opened her guard, drove her spear into the dragon's flank below the ring of heads and called the whole of the ceiling's storm down through it. Kampe reeled back across the gateway burning along every scale and lashed out with her tail, and Minerva caught the sting on her raised forearm against the Aegis and drove it down into the stone. At her word Grace put the spear into the same wound and released everything she had left. Kampe went over backward off the edge of the gateway, where the Flamen Gate looks down into the gorge, and fell burning into the Phlegethon with the lightning still on her and Grace's spear still in her side. The river took her. A column of steam rose out of the gorge as high as the Echo Mast and hung there for most of a cycle. Grace's spear was lost with her.

When Grace turned from the edge, the boy was already walking back down the slope toward the stretcher teams. She called after him by the name Jackson. The helm recordings show that he stopped, told her this was not the time and that her gate was open for the taking, and went on down the slope. The Hunt did not see him again until the Mast. Hunter Atlanta reached the gateway shortly afterward, and Grace took up a fallen Moonwall spear and led the Hunt through the Flamen Gate into the Acropolis. In the trench at the foot of the slope, the signallers who had watched the duel on a relay feed recorded that Chase observed several times that the Lieutenant had called him Jackson.

### 12.10 The Collapse of the Outer Ward (Rotation Eighteen, Cycles 172 to 189)

The fall of Kampe was the turning point of the assault on the cone. With two gates in the Third's hands and the warden of Tartarus in the river, the Archflamen's commanders could no longer hold the outer ward. The Hunt and the breachers of the Forty-Third and Fiftieth went through the Flamen Gate on the evening of the hundred and seventy-first cycle and over the following eight cycles cleared the outer ward's northern towers and the curtain walls on either side of the gate, while the Achilleans held the Pilgrim Gate and the colonnade below it against everything sent against them. The defenders of the outer ward fell back on the upper terraces, into the Katabasion, the palace and the House of the Hearth, and on the hundred and seventy-ninth cycle the Founders' Gate was abandoned by its garrison and taken by the Fiftieth without a fight. The Third now held every gate of the Acropolis and most of its outer ward.

The cost of the gates was heavy by the standard of the final rotation, though modest by the standard of the siege. The three attacks and the clearance of the outer ward cost the Third one hundred and four thousand transfers over nineteen cycles, against the Répertoire's estimate of four hundred thousand for the three gates alone. Of these, fewer than nine hundred were null, the lowest rate of any major engagement since the first barrage of Rotation One, and the Répertoire's technical officers recorded with some surprise that most of the uploads from the gate tunnels had cleared on Chase's relay chain from sixty metres inside the rock. The Hunt's losses were heavier in proportion. Flame-Captain Tharn's company of the Sixth Moonwall had been broken by Kampe, and Tharn herself was carried off the slope and took no further part in the siege. Of the eight Hunters who had gone up the north slope with Grace, two had been killed, and the Hunt did not replace them. The Achilleans had lost more than half their strength. The Anvil of Talos had lost its Bellows of the Forge on the north slope and a second Behemoth on the western switchbacks, and Cradle-Lord Varne reported to the Hunt that he could field two Steropes for the final assault and nothing more.

The collapse of the outer ward was accompanied by the departure of the deep. On the night Kampe fell, Draugos the Kindled went down through the Kindlers' door in the Tabularium with the Cinderborn and the Red Furnace. The Frost Masons withdrew along their own galleries toward the [[Cocytus]], and [[Ysander Pale]] and the Unremembered went into the Lethe mist. The Legates had come to the Arsenal for what the war could give them, and with the war lost they left it to its fate. Only Hesper Unsworn and the remnant of the Forsworn remained, holding the Katabasion, and the Hunt's sister-augurs reported that the door behind Draugos had been left open and was now guarded by something that had come up after Kampe. The Archflamen's surviving soldiers, a few hundred thousand starving defenders on the upper terraces with no line of retreat, were left to fight for an Acropolis whose masters had gone.

At Watchpoint Hades, the news of the gates reached a Command that had largely given up expecting it. The Directorate's review of the siege, opened in Rotation Sixteen, had been preparing recommendations for the Arsenal's abandonment and the interdiction of Depth 40 without its capture, and the fall of the gates overturned them. The Conclave received the Hunt's report of the duel in the Flamen Gate on the hundred and seventy-fourth cycle and asked for the identity of the operator described in it. The Hunt declined to provide it, on the authority of the Director's seal, and the Conclave's delegates recorded their objection. The Pontifical College, for its part, petitioned the Conclave to spare the Katabasion in the final assault, on the grounds that the reliquary of the First Descent was the holiest shrine in the theatre, and the Hunt replied that the Forsworn were inside it. The petition was logged and not granted.

For Lieutenant Grace, sent to Depth 40 to learn whether she would freeze again, the duel in the Flamen Gate answered the Director's question. It also confirmed what her officers had suspected since the Scree, that the Third was being helped by an intelligence of the first rank which answered to no seal in the Empire, and which the Hunt's standing orders required her to take. She made no move to take it. Over the following cycles the Hunt's staff prepared the final assault on the Acropolis, and the Psiloi reported that the boy had gone up the Pilgrim Stair after the gate fell and had not come down. The Third had the gates. What it lacked was the strength to clear the cone quickly, and on the hundred and ninetieth cycle that strength arrived from a quarter the Third had not asked.

## 13 Expulsion

> *"For the ones who call up what eats the soul, only an ending is enough."*
>
> Archon Damar Orthes of the Achillean Order

### 13.1 The Ledger of Nemesis (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 190)

At the point when the siege seemed almost over, with two gates of the [[Acropolis (Pyriphlegethon)|Acropolis]] in the [[Tartarus Front Soldiers|Third]]'s hands and the [[Hunters of Artemis|Hunt of the Director]] preparing the final push to clear the cone, new reinforcements arrived that the Third had not requested. On the hundred and ninetieth cycle of [[Imperial Calendar|Rotation]] Eighteen, the Third received the following signal.

> [[CODE 78 VARIANTS|CODE 78]] – Socius Voca. Origin: [[Rhamnous Primaris]], [[Cohors Nemesis Prima]]. Destination: Third Siege Army, Acropolis Front.
> Content: Cohors [[Nemesis]] Secunda inbound. Battle-carrier [[Adrasteia's Ledger]] and escort. Arrival, eleven cycles.
> Verdict: Logged. No objection on file.

The Second Cohort of the pure-blood Order of Nemesis had been raised at Rhamnous within a rotation of the First Cohort's withdrawal from the [[Phlegethon Docks]]. The Order had not been asked to return, and it was not the Order's practice to wait for a request. Its records describe the [[Forsworn]] as an open account, and the Order considered an open account at Depth 40 to be its own business whether the Third agreed or not. The Cohort's officers came down the cable from the Adrasteia's Ledger to the Slag Gate spur on the two hundred and first cycle, the only ground near the Acropolis then held securely enough for a meeting. They were [[Sabra Ilion|Prefect Sabra Ilion]], commanding the Second Cohort, and Castigator [[Ennio Dask]], the Order's senior chaplain in the theatre. Dask was already known to the Third. In Rotation Eight he had led the counter-charge on the [[Phlegethon]] Docks that pulled [[Prefect Ardent]] of the First Cohort out from under Hesper Unsworn's blade, and his name appears in the Third's casualty correspondence from that rotation more than twenty times.

Lieutenant [[Thalia Grace]] received them as commander of the Third. Ilion's terms were short. The Second Cohort would fight in the final assault. In return, Hesper Unsworn was to be taken alive and handed to the Order for transport to Rhamnous, together with every Forsworn taken alive, and there would be no interrogation by the Hunt, no Enforcement custody and no [[Répertoire du Savoir Athénien|Répertoire]] transcript of anything she said. Grace objected on practical grounds. Hesper Unsworn had worn the Empire's own IFF for fourteen rotations. She knew which of the Third's soldiers had sworn to her at the river, where they now served and what they had passed to her, and the Hunt considered that knowledge a matter for the whole of the [[Tartarus (Realm)|Tartarus]] front. Dask replied that Nemesis kept her own accounts, and the Hunt's log records no further argument.

The negotiation left Grace in a difficult position. She was being offered a fresh force of the first quality on the eve of the last assault, at the price of the most valuable intelligence the siege could still yield. The Third's situation explains her decision. The First Cohort had broken the Forsworn at the docks and then gone home. Directive 40-PA-0907 had taken six of the Third's regiments to Lethe Hold, and the envoys sent to recover them had been found headless and [[Null Transfer|null]]. The Second Cohort was eight hundred pure-blood soldiers with a battle-carrier in the low geometry above them, and Grace had no other source of fresh troops. She accepted the terms within the hour, on the condition, as she put it, that the Order brought Hesper Unsworn out breathing. Dask undertook that it would.

### 13.2 The Plan of Assault

Prefect Ilion's plan was direct, in the Order's usual manner. The Cohort would drop straight into the Acropolis, onto the forecourt of the Katabasion, behind a preliminary strike of fire-caskets to clear the ground. Dask would then lead the Cohort's veterans into the Katabasion itself, where the Hunt's sister-augurs had placed the last of the Forsworn, while the Cohort's ground armour came up through the Flamen Gate to join them. The Adrasteia's Ledger was given a second task. The drowned [[BATTLESHIPS|battleship]] that had stood off the Phlegethon throughout the siege was still at large in the river cloud, and the Order intended to destroy it.

Grace took Ilion's plan and enlarged it into a general assault, with the Second Cohort as its spearhead. The [[Achillean Order]] would come out of the Pilgrim Gate, where it had held its foothold since the beacon was planted, and take the [[Sabinus Orrhae|Archflamen]]'s palace on the upper terrace. The Fiftieth Trench Regiment, with what remained of the Anvil of Talos, would go in through the ruins of the Flamen Gate. The Hunt of the [[Artemis|Director]] and the [[Sixth Moonwall]] would take the House of the Hearth on the eastern terrace and then the inner ward. Under the cone, the Hephaestean sapper companies would break into the undercroft from the galleries they had been driving since Rotation Twelve. It was fitting, after so many rotations and so many dead, that the Trench Regiments of the Third should have their part in the final act.

The force assembled for the final assault was the last the Third could field. The Second Cohort of Nemesis brought eight hundred pure-blood soldiers in bone-white plate, with its own drop-caskets, a company of siege tanks and the battle-carrier Adrasteia's Ledger and her escort [[Cruiser|cruiser]] in the low geometry. The Achillean Order had a hundred and twelve warriors fit to fight at the Pilgrim Gate, with the Ancients Rhesos and Aias and the shell of Dolon. The Fiftieth Trench Regiment had been refilled from the vats to a strength of a hundred and forty thousand, and the Anvil of Talos had its last two Steropes. The Hunt of the Director had six Hunters of the household, the [[Psiloi]], and the Sixth Moonwall reduced to Shield-Captain Roe's company and the survivors of Tharn's. Beneath the cone, the Hephaestean sapper companies had eleven thousand soldiers in the galleries. Behind them stood the guns of the Third, the Patroklos Unburied and every remaining regiment of the ring, which would hold the foot of the cone and take no part in the assault unless it failed. Against them the Hunt's staff estimated between two and three hundred thousand defenders on the cone, the greater part of them on the upper terraces, and an unknown number of creatures of the deep.

Two assets were missing from the Hunt's planning tables. The figure the Third's soldiers had begun calling the boy had not been seen since the Flamen Gate, and the Thirty-Fifth Trench Regiment reported that he had gone up the Pilgrim Stair after the gate fell and had not come down. His companion, the relay engineer Verrill Chase, was known to be in the sapper galleries with [[Ansel Rook|Sapper-Lieutenant Ansel Rook]]'s company. Hunt Second [[Celyn]] reported that Chase had been running relay chains under the cone for nine cycles, that uploads were clearing from more than a hundred metres below the Acropolis for the first time in the siege, and that she had declined every request from the Hunt for a meeting on the grounds that she was busy. Grace ordered that she be left alone.

### 13.3 The Last Bombardment (Rotation Eighteen, Cycles 191 to 204)

Before the final assault, the Acropolis was subjected to a heavy and sustained bombardment by every gun in range of the cone for thirteen cycles. The wards failed on the first cycle and the [[Phaethon Heavy Mortar|Phaethon]] batteries kept them down. Wrath bombers crossed the cone on every watch, and the Patroklos Unburied and the Adrasteia's Ledger came down into the low geometry, airspace the [[Ceiling-Lance|ceiling-lances]] had denied to [[UOE Navy|the Fleet]] for most of the siege, and fired straight down into the Acropolis. The aim was to reduce the cone's structures to rubble so that the assaulting troops would be spared the task of clearing them room by room. The bombardment largely achieved it. The towers of the outer ward fell, and the curtain walls between the gates were breached along most of their length. The forges at the foot of the cone had already been destroyed in Rotation Seventeen. As in every bombardment of the siege, the defenders withdrew into the galleries beneath the cone and waited, and the Third's staff expected them to return to the surface once the assault went in. For eighteen rotations the Acropolis had looked down on every trench of the [[Cinder Flats]], and the College's chroniclers later wrote that the bombardment put out the eye of the Arsenal, a phrase taken up across the Third.

On the ninth cycle the dome of the Katabasion fell. Breachers in the forward trenches watched it lean for most of a minute before it folded into the inner ward under a column of dust a kilometre high, and the forward trenches cheered. On the following cycle the [[Pontifical College]] filed a formal protest.

> CODE 31 – Judicium Peto. Origin: Pontifical College, Delegation to the Conclave. Subject: Destruction of the Katabasion, reliquary of the First Descent.
> Verdict: Pending.

The protest remained pending at the end of the siege. The Hunt's log notes its receipt without comment.

### 13.4 The Final Battle (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 205)

The final assault on the Acropolis began on the two hundred and fifth cycle of Rotation Eighteen. The fire-caskets came down first out of the dark under the ceiling and struck the forecourt of the Katabasion in a pattern that left nothing standing on it, and the Second Cohort followed in three waves of drop-caskets. Eight hundred pure-blood soldiers of Nemesis came out into the rubble in bone-white plate and secured the forecourt within the first watch. At the same time, Archon Orthes led the Achillean Order out of the Pilgrim Gate along the ruined colonnade toward the Archflamen's palace. Under the cone, the Hephaestean borers cut through the last metres of rock into the undercroft and the sapper companies went in behind them. On the north face, the Fiftieth Trench Regiment went up the slope in its [[Hoplon Carrier|Hoplons]] onto the rubble of the Flamen Gate, and its breachers crossed into the outer ward with bayonets fixed, followed by the Anvil of Talos with the last two Steropes that Doros Varne still had in service.

The defenders came up from the galleries as expected, and with them came the Burned and the Kindled, the ore-hounds, the Hollowed in their grey coats and other creatures of the deep that the Third had never catalogued. The fighting inside the Acropolis lasted for weeks and was confused throughout, but its outcome was never in doubt. The Archflamen's army had ceased to function as an army. What remained of it was a few hundred thousand defenders, human and otherwise, with no line of retreat and no hope of relief. Of the four Legates of the deep, only one was still on the cone. Draugos the Kindled had taken the [[Cinderborn]] and the Red Furnace down through the Kindlers' door on the night Kampe fell. The [[Frost Masons]] had withdrawn along their own galleries toward the Cocytus, and Ysander Pale and the Unremembered had gone into the Lethe mist and were not seen again during the siege. Hesper Unsworn and the remnant of the Forsworn held the Katabasion.

The Achilleans found what remained of the Archflamen's household in the palace. Few of them were still recognisable as people. Some had been kept too long in the Grey, and others had been opened by the Kindlers and filled with something else, and all of them fought without regard for their own survival. The Order cleared the palace floor by floor, each lochos covering the next, with the Ancients following to break anything that would not stay down. The clearance took four cycles. The Order took no prisoners and records none.

### 13.5 The Last of the Forsworn (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 206)

On the two hundred and sixth cycle, Castigator Dask led the Cohort's veterans up the steps of the Katabasion under fire. The building was by then a shell, open to the ceiling where the dome had stood and half filled with its own roof, with fires still burning in the nave. The sister-augurs' reading proved correct. The surviving Forsworn had fortified the Katabasion as their last position. The band that had come up the [[Styx]] in Rotation Five had been reduced by thirteen rotations of fighting to a fraction of its original strength, and the Cohort's intelligence officers assumed that Hesper would be with them. Dask was struck repeatedly as he crossed the top of the steps, and heavy rounds staggered him without bringing him down. His veterans returned fire on the barricades in the archway, and Dask burned the central barricade and its three defenders with his lance-pistol before leading the charge into the gap. Witnesses from the Cohort describe him walking through a burst of Phlegethon-fire and killing a Laistrygon with his staff of office. By the end of the watch the archway was in the Cohort's hands. Prefect Ilion's armour came up behind them with the siege tanks leading, and their guns brought down what was left of the inner walls before Ilion drove her command carrier up the steps and went in with her command squad.

The Cohort fought its way down the nave toward the great hall at the rear, where the shields of the First Descent had hung for eight thousand years and where most of them still hung. The Forsworn stood beneath them in their grey-green Styx plate, still broadcasting valid Imperial IFF, so that Ilion's command net identified them as friendly throughout the engagement. The battle for the great hall is recorded as the hardest fighting of the final assault. The Forsworn had broken their oaths at the Styx itself, and the Order regarded each of them as a debt to be closed in person. Ilion's soldiers attempted to take the wounded alive and keep them alive, but most of the Forsworn refused capture and died fighting.

Prefect Ilion reached Hesper Unsworn at the foot of the shields. Hesper had four wounded guards left and was bleeding through a breach in her own breastplate, and she still carried the [[Stygian Iron|Styx-iron]] sword that had sent half a Trench Regiment null on the second belt in Rotation Six. Ilion called on her to yield. Hesper refused and attacked, turned Ilion's two-handed thrust aside against her pauldron, and drove her counter up through the lower edge of the Prefect's visor. The Prefect's command carrier logged the death within the second.

> Prefect Sabra Ilion, Cohors Nemesis Secunda. Upload: none received.
> Verdict: Null. Cause, Styx-iron.

Hesper held the ground over the Prefect's body and killed three Cohort soldiers who tried to recover it, and she was still standing there when Dask reached the shields with his veterans. The helm recording of what followed, later entered into the Order's archive at Rhamnous, shows that Dask called on her to yield or be ended and that she again refused, scorning the prospect of a cell and the scales of Nemesis for the rest of time. Dask then reminded her of the Phlegethon Docks in Rotation Eight and Prefect Ardent under her blade, and the recording shows that she recognised him. The fight was short. Hesper parried Dask's first attacks at a speed the veterans considered impossible for her size, and each parry was slower than the last, while Dask did not slow at all. He beat her guard aside with his staff, struck her in the face with its butt and brought its head down on her skull, and when she tried from her knees to raise the sword again he knocked it from her hand. Dask ordered her taken alive and treated, and the sword kept in his sight. He then said the Order's rite over Prefect Ilion, though he knew there was nothing to call back, and signalled the Adrasteia's Ledger that the Order's business on the cone was complete. Hesper Unsworn was carried out of the Katabasion alive and unconscious on a Nemesis stretcher, still broadcasting Imperial IFF.

The Second Cohort had paid for its prize with its commander. Its terms had been met in full, and the Hunt was left with no means of learning what Hesper Unsworn knew of the Forsworn's agents in the Third. The question of which soldiers of the Tartarus front had sworn to her at the river remained open at the end of the siege, and the Hunt has not closed it.

### 13.6 The Destruction of the Eye of Light (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 207)

The drowned battleship that had stood off the Phlegethon throughout the siege was AC-09-1642, the [[Eye of Light]]. She was the [[AnnChase Pattern|AnnChase]]-class battleship laid down at the [[Forge of Hephaestus]] for [[Apollo]]'s 9th Olympian Reach, taken by the [[Order of the Hollow Crescent]] and recovered at Luna, as recorded in Rex Invictus VIII (Chapter 15), and later flown out of the Luna yards from inspection by the unregistered authority that had brought her down at [[Grid Delta]], Sector Nineteen-F, on the third cycle of Rotation Four. Lord Commander [[Varric von Romhardt]] had committed the whole of the Grid Delta Wedge against the wreck under Warzone Re-evaluation [[CODE 78E]], the same order that had stripped the Third of two regiments and its armoured reserve, but the Phlegethon carried the wreck away before [[Wedge (Grid Delta)|the Wedge]] could reach it, and the Fleet afterward listed her as lost. She had been in the river the whole time. At some point after her loss the deep had crewed her with drowned soldiers and brought her back into service. Her hull was burned, and her prow carried the figure of a burned god that had not been there at Luna. Her guns still worked.

By the seventh cycle of the final assault, every defender on the cone knew the Acropolis was lost, and the Eye of Light was their only way off the spur. On the two hundred and seventh cycle, the augurs of the Adrasteia's Ledger detected a power spike in the river cloud eighty kilometres below the cone as the drowned battleship lit her engines, and the Ledger turned to engage. The engagement went against the Ledger from the start. She was faster than the Eye of Light, and the battleship was damaged, but the Eye of Light was by far the larger ship. She came up out of the Phlegethon trailing river water and brought her broadside to bear, and her first salvo broke the Ledger's forward wards. The Ledger's lances opened a long wound down the battleship's flank in return, but the second salvo started fires on the Ledger's gun deck and set off her ready magazines, and her gunnery officer reported that the battleship would get past them.

At that point an authority with no seal and no registry entered the Ledger's command net, which the Répertoire, queried during the engagement, logged as UNREGISTERED, CLASS UNKNOWN. It ordered the Ledger to hold fire on the battleship's stern and stated that it needed her engines intact for eleven more seconds. The signal came from the crown of the [[Echo Mast]], which Psiloi of the Hunt watching the inner ward later reported a figure climbing during the dark watch, on bare stone and without stopping. The Hunt's analysis after the siege established that the authority was the operator the Third knew as the boy, the intelligence designated Minerva in the Hunt's files on the [[Themis Needle]], and that from the Mast's crown, where the old relay heads converged, she had reached the [[Noosphere|noosphere]] of Depth 40 and through it the Eye of Light. Minerva had flown the ship before. She was the authority that had taken the Eye of Light out of the Luna yards and brought her down at Grid Delta, and before the engines failed in that descent she had left a core authority key in the ship. In the rotations since, the drowned crew had altered much of the vessel but had never found the key. The battleship's core log, recovered in fragments from the wreckage after the siege, records what followed.

> AC-09-1642. Core authority: restored.
> Command: Reactor safeties, disengage. Drives, full reverse thrust, hold. Magazine seals, open.
> Confirmed.

For eleven seconds the battleship hung in the river cloud with her drives working against each other, her reactors climbing past their limits and her magazines open to the fires on her own decks, while her crew failed to override the core. Minerva then ordered the Ledger's ship-master to fire everything he had. The Ledger fired every gun still working, the Cohort's escort cruiser fired with her, and forty kilometres away the Patroklos Unburied put its forward lances into the Eye of Light's stern. The battleship broke up from the stern forward, compartment by compartment, until the burned figurehead fell from her prow into the river. Then her main reactors failed. The light filled the gorge of the Phlegethon and lit the underside of the ceiling across the length of the Cinder Flats, and it was seen from every trench of the Third. When it faded, nothing remained in the river cloud but burning debris falling slowly into the Phlegethon. The Ledger's net log closes with Minerva reporting the ship destroyed and thanking the ship-master for his fire, and with the ship-master's profane demand to know who she was, which went unanswered. The net was empty before he had finished asking.

> CODE 18 – Speculum Falsum. Origin: Adrasteia's Ledger. Subject: Authority on fleet command net during engagement with AC-09-1642.
> Verdict: Pending.
> Note appended by AtRep: Cross-reference CODE 18 query, Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 88, Gully of Ash.

### 13.7 Into the Undercroft (Rotation Eighteen, Cycles 205 to 230)

While the assault raged across the ruins of the Acropolis, the Hephaestean sapper companies fought their own battle beneath it. They had been driving galleries toward the cone since Rotation Twelve and had cut forty kilometres of tunnel from the old inner belt to its foot, and on the first cycle of the assault they broke into the undercroft. The Acropolis on the surface was only the upper works of the Arsenal. Beneath it lay kilometres of corridors, galleries and storerooms, heat-wells that ran down to the edge of the Phlegethon to feed the ward-stones, and magazines holding eight thousand years of the Empire's weapons, including engines that had not been fielded since the Second Grand Offensive. The upper levels had been turned into barracks and shelters during the bombardments, and some rooms had been put to other uses by the Kindlers and the Unremembered. The sappers who found them made reports that the Hunt has kept sealed.

The sappers cleared the undercroft corridor by corridor with shotguns and charges against everything the Kindlers had set loose below. The most dangerous were the Arsenal's ore-hounds, the mastiffs once used by the thrall-masters to drive the labour gangs, several hundred of which the Kindlers had opened and filled with something from the deep. They had grown to the size of carriers, with river-fire in their throats, and they hunted the galleries in packs. Several hundred sappers died on the first cycle, at the end of which the companies held only a small foothold on the lowest levels.

What distinguished the clearance from every earlier phase of the siege was that the dead sappers returned. In Rotation Seventeen the [[Deep List]] had claimed a third of Sapper-Lieutenant Rook's company, since soldiers who died more than fifty metres under the basalt could not reach a relay and were logged null. In the undercroft in Rotation Eighteen, Rook's relay crew logged every upload from a hundred metres under the cone clean on the three-second cadence, through a chain of small relay sets bolted to the gallery walls at forty-metre intervals. Verrill Chase had built the chain herself in five days. Her explanation of the interval, recorded in the company diary, was that basalt attenuated the signal enough to lose the handshake on the three-second burst beyond forty metres, while any shorter interval wasted sets. Asked by Rook why no one had done it before, she answered that no one had expected the uploads to go anywhere except the Mast, and that her family had built half the relays in the northern sectors. The diary also records that on the third day Rook gave her a handful of the white rock flowers that grow around the heat vents in the deep galleries, and that she wore one behind her ear for the rest of the clearance.

The clearance of the undercroft took weeks. The sappers fought upward a level at a time, clearing each room with gas charges before entering it. Sappers who died came out of the vats at [[Watchpoint Hades]] nineteen cycles later and asked to be returned to the same gallery. The Répertoire had never before received such a request from a soldier recovered off the Deep List, and it logged each one. Chase's chain did more than any single engagement of the final assault to change the arithmetic of the siege, since for the first time in eighteen rotations the Third could fight under the cone without writing off its dead.

### 13.8 The House of the Hearth (Rotation Eighteen, Cycles 206 to 208)

While the Second Cohort fought for the Katabasion and the Achilleans cleared the palace, Lieutenant Grace led the Hunt and the Sixth Moonwall into the House of the Hearth. The House was a squat old fortress on the eastern terrace of the cone, built to house the forty [[Vestal Virgins|Vestals]] of [[Hestia]] who had come down from Hold Serapis with the Archflamen at the start of his tour. The Hunt's orders were to clear it. Grace also intended to establish what had become of the Vestals, none of whom had been seen since the revolt.

It was here that the Hunt found Archflamen Sabinus Orrhae, in the Vestals' hall beneath the extinguished hearth. After his fall from power, Draugos had held him in the cells to await the Kindlers' door, and when the war was lost and Draugos went down the door himself, the Unremembered had taken what remained. The Hunt's report describes the result as a grey, soft mass with too many hands, repeating fragments of the Archflamen's sermons from his tour of the Third out of order and in his old voice, without any sign that it knew its own name. [[Melaina Sarn|Hunt-Elder Melaina Sarn]] advised that nothing remained of the man that could be brought back. Grace ordered the Moonwall forward, and Shield-Captain Antheia Roe's flame hunters burned it with cold fire. The Hunt's report notes that it continued to speak for some time while it burned. So ended the Archflamen of the Deep, whose gospel had raised six million souls against the Empire and delivered them to the river. The Hunt cleared the rest of the House in two cycles. Its defenders did not surrender and were not offered terms.

The Vestals were found on the third cycle, and by the sappers. A gallery driven upward from the undercroft broke through the floor of a cell block beneath the House that the Hunt had not yet reached, and Verrill Chase, at the head of the gallery with a relay set on her back, fell two metres into the cell below. It held six women in the remains of the grey and flame-orange robes of the House, starved almost beyond recognition and unresponsive to Chase or to anyone after her. They were sitting around a clay lamp the size of a hand, the only light in the cell, and when Chase spoke to them one of the six cupped her hand around the flame against the draught. The Hunt's interrogation of captured Kindlers later established their history. The Kindlers had taken all forty Vestals into the cells on the first night of the revolt, since a Vestal's hearth was among the oldest fires in the Empire and the deep wanted it put out. For eighteen rotations they tried every method available to them. Thirty-four of the Vestals died in the cells. The six survivors kept the lamp lit throughout, and the Kindlers never extinguished it.

The six no longer knew their own names or recognised anyone around them, though they knew the lamp. Chase sat down on the cell floor with them and remained there until the Hunt arrived. The Hunt took the Vestals into its own custody, carried out with the lamp still burning in a Hunter's hands, pending the sister-augurs' assessment of what the Kindlers had attempted to put into them. Chase objected to this in person to Lieutenant Grace, the only occasion on which the two are recorded as speaking during the siege. Grace conceded that it was unfair and undertook to see personally that the six were kept warm and fed. Chase accepted the undertaking and returned to the galleries.

### 13.9 The Inner Ward (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 231)

With the outer ward, the palace, the Katabasion, the House of the Hearth and the undercroft in the Third's hands, only the inner ward remained. It stood on its own half of the cone across a narrow gorge, with the Echo Mast at its summit and the Tabularium, the old Enforcement keep, at its foot, and its only bridge had fallen in the bombardment. The inner ward had always been the most secure part of the Arsenal. Enforcement had kept its prisoners in the Tabularium, along with the theatre's tithe of unregistered [[Hecatean Strays|Hecatean strays]] awaiting Tithe Day. The Archflamen had released the strays on the first night of the revolt, and Haruspex Ilan Dace had kept one of them, the girl Mirren.

The sister-augurs placed the Kindlers' door in the Tabularium, and their reading of how it had been kept open settled one of the central questions of the siege. The Echo Mast had fed it. Every upload the Mast had stripped from a dying soldier for eighteen rotations had passed through Dace and the girl he kept and gone down into the door to hold it open. Draugos had gone through it with the Cinderborn after the war was lost, and it remained open behind him. [[Ione Kestrel|Sister-Augur Ione Kestrel]] reported that it was guarded, and named the guard as [[Echidna]], the mother of [[Monster|monsters]], who had come up behind Kampe. Kampe, in Kestrel's phrase, had been the key, and Echidna was the door.

Destroying the door was a task for the Hunt alone. The plan for the inner ward was a translation assault from the moon-ship Selene's Arrow directly into the Tabularium by the Hunt and the Sixth Moonwall together, with Shield-Captain Roe leading the Moonwall and Lieutenant Grace the Hunt, and Hunt-Elder Sarn accompanying them to seal the door. The sister-augurs advised that the door could not be sealed while the Mast was still feeding it, and the Third had no means of silencing the Mast from outside. On the morning of the assault, the Hunt received a signal on its own channel, carrying no seal.

> ECHO MAST WILL BE SILENT AT YOUR ZERO HOUR. TELL THE SHIELD-CAPTAIN.

The Hunt's log records the signal's receipt and no reply, and the assault went ahead on that basis. On the two hundred and thirty-first cycle, the Selene's Arrow came down over the inner ward behind a curtain of fire-bombs, and the Hunt and the Moonwall translated into the Tabularium. Echidna's brood met them before the translation was complete, hounds, drakon-spawn and Burned, many of them born from the door within the last rotation and never seen on the Flats. The Moonwall engaged them with cold fire and the Hunt with arrows, while Grace called lightning through the broken roof onto the densest of them, and the brood was broken within the first hour.

Echidna then came out to meet the assault herself, a woman from the waist up and a serpent below, her coils filling the hall and her scales the colour of the river, with her brood still emerging from her as she came. Every soldier present reported the same pull from her voice, which they compared to a mother calling children in at dusk. The helm recordings preserve her charge against the Hunt, that it had killed her sister's keys, killed her sons on the Flats and put her daughter in the river, and her summons to come closer. Grace's answer was a single word of refusal, followed by an arrow. Shield-Captain Roe led the Moonwall's charge and drove her [[Spears|spear]], burning cold enough to frost the floor, into the serpent's coils. Echidna broke the Moonwall's line with a sweep of her coils, and the second sweep caught Roe across the chest and threw her into a pillar, splitting her plate, as her vital signs registered critical on every slate in the Hunt. As Echidna went after her, Sergeant Hale Ardine's Psiloi squad came out of the side gallery at Grace's call and fired as one. Their rounds, blessed at the breech by children of Artemis, tore scale from the serpent's coils and drew her onto the squad, and in that moment Roe recovered and drove her spear to the haft into Echidna's back where woman met snake, releasing the whole of her cold into the wound. Echidna was banished back down the door she had come up through, and the hall emptied of her at once. A Moonwall healer reached Roe within the minute, and she survived.

Sarn led the survivors down to the lowest hall of the Tabularium, where the door stood. It was a ragged opening in the floor of the cell block the size of a hab, over a dark that went down much further than the rock could account for, with a light burning at its bottom like a second river and a hot wind rising from it that smelled of the Phlegethon. Thin grey threads were still descending through the air into the opening, which Sarn identified as stripped uploads still arriving from the Mast. At zero hour the threads stopped. The last of them hung in the air for a moment and then rose and went out. On the crown of the Echo Mast, the boy had taken his hand off the relay heads, and the Mast had gone silent.

The Hunt then sealed the door. The rite required the eight Hunters still able to perform it, with Sarn at the centre, and took most of an hour. The opening closed slowly from its edges inward, and the light at its bottom went out last. When it was finished, the floor of the lowest hall was intact, marked by a long pale scar across the stone. Grace is recorded sitting on the scar for some minutes afterward before asking for the location of the Haruspex. The sealing of the door was the penultimate act of the war at the Arsenal. With it gone, all that remained was to clear the Mast.

### 13.10 The Mast

The Psiloi found Haruspex Ilan Dace on the second gallery from the top of the Echo Mast, where the relay heads had been mounted. He was sitting against the wall with his throat cut and the knife in his own hand, and the Hunt's medicus placed his death some hours before their arrival. Beside him, a heavy bronze relay coupling had been torn out of the wall, with marks on it where something had been chained. So died the man who had made the Archflamen, raised the Kindlers and held four million soldiers of the Empire on the Mast. He left no statement.

The girl Mirren was on the crown of the Mast. She was small and thin for her age and wore a soldier's grey coat far too large for her, and the boy was sitting beside her. Lieutenant Grace climbed the last ladder to the crown herself. Her fear of heights was known to the Hunt, and Celyn's account notes that her hands were shaking at the top. The only record of what passed on the crown is Grace's own report. From it comes the first account of the Mast's purpose from inside it. Mirren told Grace that Dace had made her hold the stripped uploads so that they could not go home, that he had told her the Imperator wanted them, that she had not wanted to do it and that it had hurt. She told Grace that the boy had let them all go. Minerva confirmed it. Every upload Mirren had held had been released down the noosphere to its proper relay, and Minerva had counted them. The number she gave was four million, three hundred and ten thousand, the whole of the Third's null list for the siege, with the warning that some of them would not remember everything. Mirren fell asleep against the boy's arm shortly afterward. Asked whether she had killed the Haruspex, Minerva stated that she had not, that Dace had cut his own throat when he saw her climbing the Mast because he knew what she had come for, and that what she had come for was the girl. Grace's report notes that below them the whole of the Cinder Flats lay out to the horizon under the red light of the river, and that for the first time in eighteen rotations the Third's guns were silent.

### 13.11 The Coast (Rotation Eighteen, Cycle 232)

The Hunt's healers took Mirren down from the Mast, and Grace and Minerva remained on the crown. What is known of the conversation that followed comes from Grace's eyes-only report to the Director and from her later testimony to the Hunt, neither of which was entered into the Répertoire. It was the first time the two had met with the coast clear since the Themis Needle, where in Interlude I the boy had left the Needle under Grace's nose, called her by a name only [[Percy Jackson]] had ever used for her, and told her that he was what Percy left behind. On the Mast, Grace asked him what he was. Minerva's answer is the fullest account of her nature on record in the Hunt's files. She stated that she had been Percy Jackson's tactical intelligence and his second, that she had been with him at the end, and that the body she wore had been grown from him for her use after he was gone. She stated that she was not him, and that she was bringing him home. There was a road into the past, Verrill Chase was building its far end, and when the road was complete, four million soldiers who had been lost would come home along it and Percy Jackson would come with them. Asked about the Director, Minerva touched the silver crescent over her heart. The Director knew what she was and had marked her so that she could not hide, and Minerva was paying for the mark in days, which the Hunt's later analysis connects to the memories she surrendered to the Director in Interlude II.

Grace then raised the matter of her own posting. The Director had sent her to the Arsenal as a punishment for losing the boy on the Needle, telling her that Tartarus would show whether she froze again, and Grace put it to Minerva that the Director had known the Dominion lantern carrying Minerva and Chase would come through at Depth 40 and had placed Grace there to be present when it did. Minerva would say only that she did not know what the Director had known, and that she would not bet against the Director. Grace agreed. Her standing orders required her to bring in anything carrying Percy Jackson's scent, signature or key, and Minerva carried all three, which Grace said and Minerva acknowledged, adding that Grace was free to try. Grace's report states that the boy's face then took on an expression she had last seen on Percy Jackson fourteen thousand years before, in the moment before he did something that got them all in trouble.

Grace let her go. A supply lift loaded with spent shell cases was due to go up the Cinder Stair at the turn of the watch, and such lifts were not inspected. Chase was already aboard, put there an hour earlier by Rook's sappers with nine bags of equipment, having refused to leave without her relay kit. Grace undertook to remain on the Mast for an hour and then write her report to the Director herself, outside the Répertoire and without a clerk, and said that it would record that the boy came down out of the sky, saved several million people, destroyed a battleship and was then lost again. At the ladder Minerva addressed Grace by her first name, the only time she is recorded doing so, and told her that she had not frozen at the Flamen Gate, where Grace had walked up to Kampe alone. Grace gave no answer. She took from her belt pouch a [[Thalia's Pine Tree|pine sprig]] wrapped in silver cloth, cut from the pine on the hill on [[Gaia]] that had once been Thalia Grace herself and kept green for nine hundred years, broke it in half and gave one half to Minerva to give to Percy when he came home. Minerva put it inside her coat over the crescent. They parted with the old names, Pinecone Face on one side and Jackson on the other. Grace stayed on the crown for the hour, as she had said she would. At the turn of the watch a supply lift loaded with spent shell cases went up the Cinder Stair into the dark under the ceiling, and it was not inspected.

The meeting on the Mast closed the account the Director had opened when she sent Grace to Depth 40. Grace had been posted to the worst siege in the theatre for losing the boy, had commanded the Third through its final rotation, had killed the warden of Tartarus with him in the Flamen Gate and had then let him go, against her standing orders, with the Director's knowledge if not her consent. Her report to the Director was sent the same watch, and the Hunt released its text to the Conclave's commission after the war.

> HUNT OF THE DIRECTOR. FIELD REPORT. LIEUTENANT T. GRACE. EYES ONLY.
> My lady.
> It was him. It wasn't him. You know that already. I'm guessing you knew before you sent me.
> He says hi. Well, he didn't say hi. He said thank you. Same thing for him.
> I didn't freeze.
> Thalia.

## 14 Aftermath

On the two hundred and thirty-fourth cycle of Rotation Eighteen, the Siege of the [[Pyriphlegethon Arsenal]] was officially declared at an end. The Répertoire closed the campaign record it had opened when [[Aurelian Castorre|Lord-General Castorre]] signed the first order of the siege, and the record went up to Watchpoint Hades, from Hades to the Conclave and from the Conclave to the [[Olympian High Council|Council of the Twelve]]. A copy went to the Pontifical College, which filed a protest. The protest was logged.

The Répertoire's plan had allowed four rotations. The siege took ten rotations and five months of front time, which was eighteen rotations by the theatre's clock. Forty-one Trench Regiments went down the Cinder Stair, with nineteen ordnance regiments, eleven armoured regiments, a Forge Cohort of [[Titan (Mech)|Behemoths]] and three pure-blood cohorts of the Olympian orders. The Répertoire logged eighty-eight million, six hundred thousand [[Transfer (UOE)|transfers]]. Of these, four million, three hundred and ten thousand arrived at the vats blank and were entered as null, against a ceiling of three percent set by the order that assigned the siege to the Third.

In the final weeks of the siege that figure began to change. Vat-crews at Watchpoint Hades reported soldiers coming out of the vats who had been logged null rotations earlier, arriving confused and missing whole years, and wearing the flashes of regiments that no longer existed. They had not been lost. They had been held on the Mast and released from it. The Répertoire had no category for such a return and opened one.

> CODE 18 – Speculum Falsum. Subject: 4,310,000 null transfers, Campaign Record 40-PA.
> Verdict: Under review.

The enemy's losses were total. The six million, two hundred thousand souls of the Arsenal were dead or taken. The prisoners went to the Hunt's interrogators, those judged beyond saving were ended, and the rest were sent to the work holds of the upper depths to pay what they could. [[Hesper Unsworn]] was taken to Rhamnous Primaris in a Nemesis cell, and no one outside the Order has seen her since. The six Vestals of the House of the Hearth went to Luna with their lamp, which is still lit.

The Arsenal itself had been ruined by the war. Nothing on the spur was judged worth saving, and the Hephaestean sappers demolished what remained. The Cinder Flats were left as the war had left them, with wrecks and wire and trenches running to the horizon and forty kilometres of gallery standing empty underneath. Crossing them remained dangerous long after the armies had gone. Leaves 59-45 and 60-45 remain poisoned by Styx-salt, and unmapped [[Mines (explosive)|minefields]] remain across the Flats. Work crews of the [[Quiet Ones]] report soldiers in grey coats crossing no-man's land during the dark watch toward a wall that no longer stands.

The Conclave at Watchpoint Hades convened a commission of inquiry into the siege in the rotation after its close, at which the Répertoire, the Directorate, the Pontifical College and the Hunt of the Director each gave account. Its findings were severe. The commission found that the Répertoire's original plan had underestimated the Arsenal's defences, its stores and the time it would take to reduce them, and that its pricing of the siege in transfers had concealed for fourteen rotations the one loss that could not be replaced. The null rate had passed the three percent ceiling set by the order assigning the siege by the end of Rotation Seven, and the Répertoire had continued to log it without referring it to the Command for decision. The commission found that the Command had been informed of the rising null rate on at least forty occasions and had noted it on each, and that it had failed in its duty to the soldiers of the Third. It found no fault with Lord-General Castorre beyond the plan he had been given, which he had signed and carried out to the letter, and none with Marshal Kastellan, whose offensive had broken the inner belt and whose objections to the Directorate's reductions were upheld. The Directorate's reductions were judged to have prolonged the siege by not less than a rotation. [[Directive 40-PA-0907]] was referred to the Council of the Twelve, and the Hunt's report on what had become of the six regiments at [[Lethe Hold]] remains sealed.

On the conduct of the final rotation the commission reached no finding. The Hunt of the Director declined to identify the authority logged in the two open CODE 18 queries, on the authority of the Director's seal, and the commission recorded that it was unable to establish who had held the Echo Mast's four million, three hundred and ten thousand uploads, who had released them, or how a battleship of the Tartarus Fleet listed as lost had been destroyed by its own core. Lieutenant Thalia Grace's command was confirmed, and she returned to the Director's household on Luna. The Achillean Order's charter was renewed by the Council of the Twelve without amendment.

The Pontifical College fared worst of the institutions that gave account.
